These are the source bytes packaged with the saved run. The job links identify the component scripts actually used; coordinator job folders also retain the earlier monorepo fallback.

# Use the workflow structure

| File | Responsibility |
| --- | --- |
| `workflow/jobs.json` | Jobs, responsible analysts, input links and parallel groups. |
| `workflow/spec.py` | Load and check these declarations; select jobs and downstream paths. |
| `jobs/<job_id>/run.R` | The selected job's R entry point. |
| `jobs/<job_id>/README.md` | Inputs, settings, outputs and connected jobs. |
| `workflow/r/` | Shared GLM, RTMB surplus-production and management-trial functions. |
| `jobs/*_report/report.qmd` | Three concise Quarto reports rendered from saved results. |
| `scripts/import-public-data.py` | Verify WCPFC releases and prepare the small albacore example. |
| `workflow/extract-public.sql` | Select common cell–quarter strata for CPUE. |
| `workflow/engine.py` | Coordination, SQLite/file adapters and execution records. |
| `Makefile` | Reader commands for container runs and reproduction. |
| `workflow/Makefile` | R calculation and Quarto report recipes inside the container. |
| `workflow/r_bridge.py` | Container guard, JSON interface and process control. |
| `scripts/offline.sh` | Repeat a preserved run locally, without retrieving sources or software. |
| `app/diagram.json` | The same job graph shown as a dependency diagram. |

## Add a job

The example uses registered jobs and three pinned analysis repositories.
Extending it requires declaring the contributing code and inputs; it does not
automatically discover an arbitrary analysis or repository.

1. Define its inputs, analyst and stage in `workflow/jobs.json`, placing parents before children. Register the job in `workflow/sources.py` (`GROUPS`) and declare its calculation libraries in `JOB_LIBRARIES`. For split execution, update the corresponding component sources and source lock as well.
2. Create `jobs/<job_id>/run.R` with `calculate(context)`. Select named inputs from `context$parents` and return a named result list. Reusable R functions belong in `workflow/r/`.
3. Record settings and contributing files in `job_settings()` and `code_record()`. Check input names, units and coverage before fitting.
4. Add the node and connections to `app/diagram.json`; the build verifies these against the calculation graph. Write a concise job guide.
5. Check the change inside the declared container. Edit page sources in `app/`; `docs/` is generated from accepted source and saved calculations.

The coordinator reads the declarations and checks saved inputs to select ready
jobs. `workflow/Makefile` calls their R entry points. The coordinator then records
the outputs and releases dependent jobs. Independent jobs can run together;
declared peer groups finish before later stages advance. Both demo views show
these job connections.

A Live request uses the existing [hosted service](cloud/README.md): it starts
`.github/workflows/live.yml`, pulls the recorded image and calls `make inside-live`
there. `cloud/run.py` retrieves the request and runs the same coordinator. The job
configuration defines the calculations; the service requires a separate deployment.

See [GitHub Actions](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/get-started/understand-github-actions)
for workflows and runners, and the
[Docker overview](https://docs.docker.com/get-started/docker-overview/)
for images and containers.

## Separate analyst repositories

The default execution uses three exact source pins in [source-lock.template.json](source-lock.template.json): preparation/CPUE owns eight jobs, assessment owns eight (including `prepare_a`/`prepare_b`), and MSE owns six. `scripts/hydrate-sources.py` checks the selected Git commits and clean source bytes, then assembles an offline snapshot. Hosted Actions hydrate before entering the preserved container. `make run`, `make check` and `make html` assemble and verify the pinned sources before entering the container; source checks stop execution if the required assembly is missing or inconsistent.

The coordinator owns shared `common.R`, the driver, input graph, SQLite adapters, source resolver, reports and software image. CPUE owns `R/cpue.R`, assessment owns `R/assessment.R`, and MSE owns `R/mse.R`. The exact per-job library declarations in `workflow/sources.py` control both loading and fingerprints. MSE preparation also loads assessment's `surplus_path`. A library revision invalidates jobs loading those bytes and their descendants; changing unrelated documentation or a component commit alone does not invalidate unchanged dependencies.

`workflow/contracts.py` checks relative CPUE indices, annual catch in tonnes, years and admissible values before assessment preparation, and the four assessment cases and required fields before MSE preparation. Records distinguish component `analysis_source` and `code_sources` from the coordinator's `source`/`execution`; inputs retain producer runs, source pins, signatures and artifact checksums. Downloaded runs preserve the component sources, source lock and origin manifest for network-disabled reproduction.

For the monorepo fallback use `PAPER_SOURCE_MODE=monorepo make run`. The [repository map](examples/analyst-repositories.yaml) is a reader guide; the executable registered pins are the JSON lock and `workflow/jobs.json`. A single host coordinates these jobs; repository separation does not establish multiple human analysts or operational fisheries use.

## Change the example

The growth control maps to `growth_rate_2`; the second assessment cases record
this fixed `r` value. Changing it invalidates those fits and their descendants.
The MSE buffer maps to `mse_buffer`; only the Buffered rule uses it. A buffer
revision retains the prepared cases, common errors and other rules. The summary
checks that every rule uses the same operating models, assumptions and recorded
random streams before producing a comparison.

The default example uses the [preserved public releases](data/public/README.md).
`scripts/import-public-data.py` checks archive and member hashes, converts the
declared units and writes the source projection. The SQL selects common observed
strata; the R model standardises catch per effort on a fixed prediction grid.
Keep source rows, transformations, units and sampling support explicit when
substituting data. The annual stock catch series is a separate input.
`scripts/seed-public-data.py` writes a PostgreSQL seed for the same snapshot.

The earlier synthetic fixture remains available with `PAPER_DATA_MODE=synthetic`.
Its generator and SQLite importer are retained for regression checks. Label any
new simulations and preserve their generating code, seed and scenario.

If software requirements change, build and check a new image version before
updating the immutable digest in the workflow. The browser and downloaded HTML
display saved results; calculations run in the container. Code, inputs and results
are preserved separately from the software image. Keep a copy of the built image
with each release; [OFFLINE.md](OFFLINE.md) gives the save and restoration commands.

## Maintain a release

Edit source files, not generated pages in `docs/`. Check changed calculations
and their input contracts in the pinned container before updating a component
source pin. The existing calculation, interface and server checks can be run
through the repository workflows; a local preserved run can be checked without
the hosting service.

Keep earlier release archives and image copies. A new release should retain its
own source pins, input snapshots, settings, outputs, execution records and
checksums. `scripts/release.py` adds local reading and execution tools to the
saved run without replacing its recorded analysis sources. Support-tool versions
can therefore differ from the saved analysis version. Change software only by
checking and recording a new image, then preserving that image as well.

## Numerical comparisons

`verify.py` compares output values at relative tolerance 1e-6 or absolute tolerance
1e-9. Assessment log-index residuals retain relative tolerance 1e-6 with absolute
tolerance 1e-8 after checking them against the observed and fitted indices. Two
near-zero assessment gradient diagnostics have magnitude and difference caps of
1e-7, conditional on matching successful fit diagnostics. These are comparison policies, not model-accuracy
guarantees. Summary and report copies must match their own source assessments
before the exceptions apply. Other values retain the general tolerances.

# Repeat and preserve the analyses

Keep the offline ZIP and the execution-image archive together. The ZIP contains
the saved run's exact inputs, settings, source snapshots, outputs and
execution records. The image contains its R packages, Python, Make and Quarto.
The offline tools are supplied with these older analysis sources so that a new
tool release does not replace the code used to produce the saved results.

## Read the example

Unpack the versioned offline ZIP and open `START-HERE.html`. Follow
the links from a result to its input records, job script and calculation library.
`code.html` displays the preserved sources. These pages use local files and need
no login or internet connection. The interactive page opens saved Quarto result PDFs;
new calculations use the commands below.

## Restore the software

Install Docker and start its engine. Use a POSIX shell, available on Linux and
macOS or through WSL on Windows. The image is for Linux AMD64; ARM hosts need
Docker's AMD64 emulation. Docker and a compatible operating system must remain
available even when the saved software is kept.

Place `fisheries-workflow-runtime.tar.gz`, `runtime-archive.json` and
`SHA256SUMS.json` in a folder named `runtime` next to the extracted analysis
folder. From the analysis folder:

```sh
sh scripts/preserve-runtime.sh load ../runtime
```

This loads your preserved archive, then checks its checksum and the restored
image's configuration and software layers before running an analysis. Use an
archive from a trusted copy of the release. A restored image can lose its registry
digest label; the tools therefore check the recorded local image identity and
layers before launching it. They record that identity alongside the original
SPC registry reference. The checks identify the preserved software, not the
scientific validity of its results.

If the recorded image is already present, loading the archive is unnecessary.
The offline runner checks it before use and stops if it is missing. It never
pulls an image or retrieves source repositories.

## Repeat and compare

From the extracted analysis folder:

```sh
sh scripts/offline.sh reproduce
sh scripts/offline.sh compare
```

The first command repeats the saved workflow using its recorded settings. The
second compares the numerical results with `reference/`, using the unchanged
comparison rules in `verify.py`. New reports and records are in `offline-runs/`.
The calculations run with network access disabled; the source folder is mounted
read-only. The runner verifies packaged files and the component source snapshots
before starting an analysis.

To run one job and prepare its required inputs:

```sh
sh scripts/offline.sh job cpue_a
```

For a single-job run download, use the job named in `REPRODUCE.txt`:

```sh
sh scripts/offline.sh reproduce cpue_a
sh scripts/offline.sh compare cpue_a
```

These commands compare that job only. Other saved results keep their original
records. An earlier download without the offline tools retains its original
instructions and requires the host tools listed there.

## Continue an analysis

`sh scripts/offline.sh run` starts the workflow with the example's default
settings. Use the same output folder to continue from its saved state; the
coordinator checks which results require recalculation. Keep original downloads
and write changes into a separate working copy. For code or input changes, use
the development route in [ADAPT.md](ADAPT.md): the preservation runner deliberately
rejects changes to packaged files.

## Keep an independent copy

If you already have the recorded image, save it for later use:

```sh
sh scripts/preserve-runtime.sh ../runtime
sh scripts/preserve-runtime.sh verify ../runtime
```

Keep the ZIP, runtime files, licences and checksums on storage independent of
the GitHub account. Their contents can be copied to a code or data archive;
GitHub release links alone do not provide an independent deposit. Each software
component retains its own licence, as described in [THIRD_PARTY.md](THIRD_PARTY.md).
Preserved copies do not require this website or the original image registry to
remain online. They still require a Docker-compatible computer.

# Fisheries workflow demonstration

[Open the demo](https://kyuhank.github.io/fisheries-workflow-demo/) · [Code by job](jobs/README.md) · [Preserved releases](https://github.com/kyuhank/fisheries-workflow-demo/releases)

Follow public catch and effort data through CPUE standardisation, stock
assessment and management strategy evaluation (MSE). Each result links to its
inputs, code, settings and software. The assessment and management models are
teaching examples and provide no advice for South Pacific albacore.

## Data and methods

The default input contains released WCPFC longline records for the FJ fleet,
2000–2022, in six fixed cell–quarter strata. A row aggregates a fleet, year,
five-degree cell and quarter; it is not an individual fishing set. Vessel IDs
are unavailable. `hooks` is reported effort (`HHOOKS × 100`) and `catch_n` is
albacore catch in numbers (`ALB_N`). The legacy `set_id` field identifies an
aggregate source row. See [data/public-source.json](data/public-source.json) for
the supplied rows and source metadata, and [data/public-data.json](data/public-data.json)
for the extraction record.

Recorded SQL extracts the CPUE rows from a SQLite snapshot. Annual South Pacific
albacore catches, in tonnes, come from a separate yearbook series; they are not
a sum of the selected fleet's catch numbers.

CPUE A fits a quasipoisson GLM with year and cell–quarter effects and a
`log(hooks / 1000)` offset. It predicts fish per 1,000 hooks in each stratum and
averages those predictions with equal fixed weights. CPUE B uses year and quarter
effects. Both indices are divided by their first-year value. The outputs preserve
nominal and standardised CPUE, prediction support, weights and model checks.
Quarter effects partly confound spatial differences on this support.
These selected released strata do not represent the whole stock; the example
supplies no confidence intervals. The selected Fiji fleet index is not a validated
stock-wide abundance measure. Synthetic vessel records remain available as
regression fixtures.

The four Schaefer assessment cases combine these indices with fixed growth
assumptions. Seeded MSE trials compare constant catch, an index rule and a
buffered rule under shared simulated conditions. Case differences and trial
fractions describe those assumptions, rather than calibrated uncertainty or risk.
All rules share a simulator catch cap of 40% of true starting biomass; the
low-biomass results are conditional on that guardrail.

## Use the demo

Plots are visible immediately. CPUE results overlay standardised and nominal rates on the same selected rows; assessment and MSE results show their main comparisons. View opens the compiled Quarto PDF, with Open PDF and Save PDF controls. Rendering reads saved numerical outputs. The three report jobs give the methods and checks.

1. Choose **View example** to inspect the saved results, or **Live run** to
   execute the R jobs and Quarto result views on GitHub Actions.
2. Select a job and open **Output**. Use **Inputs & versions** to trace its
   producing records and exact source.
3. Choose **Run full workflow**. The default snapshot ends in 2021; select 2022
   to include the final available year.
4. Change a setting and choose **Update workflow**. Affected jobs rerun;
   unchanged inputs retain their original records.

**Run** prepares a job's required inputs and stops at that job.
**Reproduce & compare** repeats the recorded workflow and compares the selected
result with its reference. **Manual handover** waits for **Confirm file transfer**
while other ready jobs continue. **Download this run** keeps source, inputs,
records and outputs before live results expire after about ten minutes.

## Run or adapt the code

The [22 job guides](jobs/README.md) show each input → method → output path.
The coordinator loads code from [pinned component repositories](jobs/README.md#separate-repositories)
and records each producing run and file checksum.

From a checkout, with Docker, Make, Python 3 and Git available:

```sh
make run                 # all jobs
make job JOB=cpue_a       # CPUE A and required inputs
```

To use another fishery, start with the source mapping and saved SQL, then
choose the CPUE covariates and prediction support explicitly. Review annual
catch units, assessment assumptions and the receiving file contracts before
running later jobs. [ADAPT.md](ADAPT.md) describes the connections and source
pins; [cloud/README.md](cloud/README.md) covers hosting.

The separate [MQMF adaptation](EXISTING-ANALYSIS.md) preserves an existing
prediction routine and historical abalone teaching data. It compares direct
and mapped inputs, then a growth-setting update. It does not reconstruct the
original CPUE standardisation.

## Preserve a run

Keep a downloaded run and its recorded execution image together. Follow the
bundle's `REPRODUCE.txt` to repeat its declared scope. The
[public-data example](https://kyuhank.github.io/fisheries-workflow-demo/downloads/fisheries-workflow-offline-1.11.5.zip)
includes local result and code pages, source archives, extraction SQL and all
22 reference outputs; its
[checksum](https://kyuhank.github.io/fisheries-workflow-demo/downloads/fisheries-workflow-offline-1.11.5.zip.sha256)
identifies the download. Open `START-HERE.html`, then follow `OFFLINE.md`.
The archived
[v1.9.1 release](https://github.com/kyuhank/fisheries-workflow-demo/releases/tag/v1.9.1)
preserves the earlier synthetic example and its separate image archive.
[OFFLINE.md](OFFLINE.md) explains restoration and offline comparison; current
source changes do not alter that preserved release.

Software attribution and licences are in [THIRD_PARTY.md](THIRD_PARTY.md).
components/assessment/R/assessment.R

Recorded component revision: 9b30d4f6d2afbaddc68ab31d83911b308037cb02
SHA-256: c807989ab5de3da1d4ac44cd7e11d84b2eee5d112c2ea74b481f896457abdf06

# B[t] is biomass before that year's catch; the final element is next year's state.
surplus_path <- function(K, r, catches) {
  B <- rep(K, length(catches) + 1L)
  for (t in seq_along(catches)) {
    B[t + 1L] <- B[t] + r * B[t] * (1 - B[t] / K) - catches[t]
  }
  B
}

# Keep the taped objective entirely inside its positive-biomass domain.
capacity_bounds <- function(r, catches) {
  feasible <- function(K) {
    B <- surplus_path(K, r, catches)
    all(is.finite(B) & B > K * 1e-6)
  }
  upper <- max(100000, max(catches) * 500)
  require_condition(feasible(upper), "No feasible Schaefer trajectory within the declared bounds.")
  lo <- 1
  hi <- upper
  for (i in seq_len(40)) {
    middle <- (lo + hi) / 2
    if (feasible(middle)) hi <- middle else lo <- middle
  }
  lower <- hi * 1.001
  require_condition(lower < upper, "No interior feasible capacity interval.")
  c(lower = lower, upper = upper)
}

schaefer_objective <- function(logK, indices, catches, r) {
  K <- exp(logK)
  B <- surplus_path(K, r, catches)[seq_along(catches)]
  difference <- log(indices) - log(B)
  logq <- sum(difference) / length(difference)
  sum((difference - logq)^2)
}

assessment <- function(inputs, r) {
  require_condition(requireNamespace("RTMB", quietly = TRUE),
                    "RTMB is required; run this job in the declared R container.")
  check_row_fields(inputs, c("year", "index", "catch_t"))
  require_condition(all(vapply(inputs, function(row) {
    is.numeric(row$year) && is.numeric(row$index) && is.numeric(row$catch_t)
  }, logical(1))), "Assessment rows require numeric years, indices and catches.")
  indices <- rows_values(inputs, "index")
  catches <- rows_values(inputs, "catch_t")
  years <- rows_values(inputs, "year")
  require_condition(length(inputs) >= 3 && all(diff(years) == 1),
                    "Assessment inputs must include consecutive annual rows.")
  require_condition(all(is.finite(indices) & indices > 0) &&
                      all(is.finite(catches) & catches >= 0) && max(catches) > 0 && r > 0 && r < 1,
                    "Assessment requires positive indices, non-negative catches and 0 < r < 1.")
  bounds <- capacity_bounds(r, catches)
  objective <- function(parameters) {
    # q is profiled, not another free parameter.
    schaefer_objective(parameters$logK, indices, catches, r)
  }
  start <- min(bounds["upper"] * 0.9,
               max(bounds["lower"] * 1.2, 4 * mean(catches) / r))
  model <- RTMB::MakeADFun(objective, list(logK = log(start)), silent = TRUE)
  fit <- stats::nlminb(model$par, model$fn, model$gr,
                       lower = log(bounds["lower"]), upper = log(bounds["upper"]),
                       control = list(iter.max = 100, eval.max = 200, rel.tol = 1e-10))
  require_condition(fit$convergence == 0, paste("Schaefer fit failed:", fit$message))
  objective_value <- as.numeric(model$fn(fit$par))
  gradient <- as.numeric(model$gr(fit$par))
  require_condition(is.finite(objective_value) && length(gradient) == 1 && is.finite(gradient),
                    "The fitted objective or gradient is not finite.")
  active_bound <- if (fit$par[1] <= log(bounds["lower"]) + 1e-7) "lower" else
    if (fit$par[1] >= log(bounds["upper"]) - 1e-7) "upper" else "none"
  # At a constrained optimum the gradient may point out of the allowed interval.
  projected_gradient <- switch(active_bound, lower = min(gradient, 0),
                                upper = max(gradient, 0), none = gradient)
  gradient_tolerance <- 1e-4
  K <- unname(exp(fit$par[1]))
  path <- surplus_path(K, r, catches)
  B <- path[seq_along(catches)]
  require_condition(all(is.finite(path) & path > 0), "The fitted Schaefer path is not feasible.")
  balance <- diff(path) - r * B * (1 - B / K) + catches
  balance_residual <- max(abs(balance))
  balance_tolerance <- 1e-8 * max(1, catches)
  require_condition(balance_residual <= balance_tolerance,
                    "The declared annual biomass recurrence does not balance.")
  q <- exp(mean(log(indices) - log(B)))
  predicted <- q * B
  series <- lapply(seq_along(years), function(i) {
    list(year = years[i], observed_index = indices[i], fitted_index = predicted[i],
         log_residual = log(indices[i] / predicted[i]),
         B_over_K = B[i] / K, harvest_rate = catches[i] / B[i],
         biomass_t = B[i], catch_t = catches[i])
  })
  list(series = series, r = r, K = K, q = q, initial_B_over_K = 1,
       next_biomass_t = unname(tail(path, 1)), feasibility_check = "Pass",
       balance_residual_t = balance_residual, balance_tolerance_t = balance_tolerance,
       balance_check = "Pass", catch_check = "Pass",
       fit_method = "RTMB::MakeADFun + nlminb", convergence = fit$convergence,
       objective_value = objective_value, objective_gradient_logK = gradient,
       projected_gradient_logK = projected_gradient, active_bound = active_bound,
       gradient_tolerance = gradient_tolerance,
       gradient_check = if (abs(projected_gradient) <= gradient_tolerance) "Pass" else "Review",
       log_index_SSE = sum(log(indices / predicted)^2),
       boundary_fit = K <= bounds["lower"] * 1.001 || K >= bounds["upper"] / 1.001,
       bounds = list(lower_K = unname(bounds["lower"]), upper_K = unname(bounds["upper"])),
       assumptions = json_array(c("Schaefer annual surplus production; initial biomass is K.",
                             "Intrinsic growth is fixed within each sensitivity case.",
                             "Only K is estimated; q is profiled from log-index residuals.",
                             "Balance checks test the declared recurrence, not an independent fit to catches.",
                             "No confidence intervals or real-stock validation are supplied.")))
}

# Fisheries workflow assessment example

These eight jobs join annual CPUE indices to catches, fit four fixed-growth
Schaefer cases, then compare and report their biomass trajectories. The
[coordinator](https://github.com/kyuhank/fisheries-workflow-demo) loads an exact
source revision and preserves the producing inputs and code bytes.

The current public-data example combines a selected Fiji fleet CPUE index with
South Pacific albacore catches. That local index is not a validated stock-wide
abundance measure. Initial biomass, fixed growth and the simple Schaefer model
are teaching assumptions; case differences are not an uncertainty interval or
stock-management advice.

Run through the coordinator in its recorded container, for example:

```sh
make job JOB=assessment_a1
```

Each job receives a named `context`; its entry point is not standalone. See
`jobs/*/README.md` for input connections. The report reads saved results without
refitting, shows the biomass comparison, and retains fit checks and source records.

[SOURCE.json](SOURCE.json) records the calculation library's baseline origin and
SHA-256. The R library and calculation entry points retain those source bytes;
the report and reader guides have been revised for the public-data example.
components/assessment/jobs/assessment_a1/README.md

# Assessment A1 (`assessment_a1`)

Fit the toy Schaefer model to index A with fixed intrinsic growth r = 0.25 and initial biomass equal to K. RTMB estimates one log(K) parameter; catchability q is profiled.

**Path:** Annual index and catches → fixed-growth Schaefer fit → biomass trajectory.

**Inputs:** Joined index and catch `rows` in `runs/prepare_a/output.json`.

**From:** [Prepare inputs A (`prepare_a`)](../prepare_a/README.md).

**Settings:** Fixed `r = 0.25`.

Biomass is reported as B/K and catches in tonnes. Growth and initial depletion are fixed sensitivity assumptions; convergence does not establish real-stock validity or uncertainty.

From the repository root, run this job and prepare any required inputs:

```sh
make job JOB=assessment_a1
```

[run.R](run.R) is the entry point. It receives the coordinator’s named
`context` and uses the recorded shared libraries; a copied script is not standalone.
See the [job guide](../README.md#run-and-inspect-a-job) for container and output options.

| Saved file | Contents |
| --- | --- |
| `runs/assessment_a1/output.json` | saved calculation result |
| `runs/assessment_a1/report.html` | result page from [shared renderer](../../workflow/reports.py) |
| `runs/assessment_a1/record.json` | producing inputs, settings, exact code and software |

**Used by:** [Compare assessments (`assessment_summary`)](../assessment_summary/README.md), [Prepare MSE (`mse_prepare`)](../mse_prepare/README.md).

[Saved report](https://kyuhank.github.io/fisheries-workflow-demo/example/assessment_a1/report.html) · [Run record](https://kyuhank.github.io/fisheries-workflow-demo/example/assessment_a1/record.json)

# Fit one log(K) with RTMB; growth and initial B/K are fixed for this case.
calculate <- function(context) {
  assessment(context$parents$prepare_a$rows, r = 0.25)
}

# Assessment A2 (`assessment_a2`)

Fit the toy Schaefer model to index A with the selected fixed intrinsic growth and initial biomass equal to K. RTMB estimates one log(K) parameter; catchability q is profiled.

**Path:** Annual index and catches → fixed-growth Schaefer fit → biomass trajectory.

**Inputs:** Joined index and catch `rows` in `runs/prepare_a/output.json`.

**From:** [Prepare inputs A (`prepare_a`)](../prepare_a/README.md).

**Settings:** `growth_rate_2` saved as `r` (default: 0.30; choices: 0.25, 0.30, 0.35).

Compare how two CPUE indices and two fixed growth assumptions affect the fitted biomass trajectory.

**Path:** Annual index and catches → fixed-growth Schaefer fit → biomass trajectory.

```{r}
# Read saved results without fitting or simulating again.
payload <- jsonlite::fromJSON("report-data.json", simplifyVector = FALSE)
result <- payload$result
record <- payload$record
rows_frame <- function(rows) {
  do.call(rbind, lapply(rows, function(row) as.data.frame(row, stringsAsFactors = FALSE)))
}
plot_series <- function(series, field, label) {
  years <- unlist(lapply(series, function(rows) vapply(rows, function(x) x$year, numeric(1))))
  values <- unlist(lapply(series, function(rows) vapply(rows, function(x) x[[field]], numeric(1))))
  colours <- c("#1378a3", "#b56435", "#7357a8", "#328363")
  plot(range(years), range(c(0, values)), type = "n", xlab = "Year", ylab = label)
  for (i in seq_along(series)) {
    lines(vapply(series[[i]], function(x) x$year, numeric(1)),
          vapply(series[[i]], function(x) x[[field]], numeric(1)),
          col = colours[(i - 1) %% length(colours) + 1], lwd = 2)
  }
  legend("topright", names(series), col = colours[seq_along(series)], lty = 1, bty = "n")
}
```

## Biomass comparison

```{r}
plot_series(result$series, "B_over_K", "Biomass / carrying capacity")
```

The trajectories describe the supplied index, catches and fixed model assumptions.
Differences among cases are not an uncertainty interval or stock-management advice.

```{r}
#| results: asis
review <- vapply(result$diagnostics, function(row) {
  isTRUE(row$boundary_fit) || (!is.null(row$convergence) && row$convergence != 0) ||
    any(vapply(c("balance_check", "feasibility_check", "gradient_check"), function(key) {
      !is.null(row[[key]]) && row[[key]] != "Pass"
    }, logical(1)))
}, logical(1))
if (any(review)) {
  cat("**Review needed:** ", paste(vapply(result$diagnostics[review], function(row) row$case,
      character(1)), collapse = ", "), ". Inspect the recorded fit checks below.\n\n", sep = "")
}
```

### Final annual values and fit checks

```{r}
endpoints <- lapply(names(result$series), function(name) {
  row <- tail(result$series[[name]], 1)[[1]]
  list(case = name, year = row$year, B_over_K = row$B_over_K,
       harvest_rate = row$harvest_rate)
})
knitr::kable(rows_frame(endpoints), digits = 3,
             col.names = c("Case", "Year", "B/K", "Harvest rate"))
checks <- lapply(result$diagnostics, function(row) {
  list(case = row$case, intrinsic_growth = row$r,
       biomass_balance_check = row$balance_check, feasibility = row$feasibility_check,
       gradient_check = row$gradient_check,
       boundary_fit = row$boundary_fit, convergence = row$convergence)
})
knitr::kable(rows_frame(checks), digits = 7,
             col.names = c("Case", "Growth (r)", "Balance", "Feasible",
                           "Gradient", "Boundary", "Convergence"))
```

Convergence, biomass balance and positive-biomass feasibility check the fitted
calculation. They do not establish biological adequacy or validate the CPUE
as a stock-wide abundance measure.




## Methods


```{r}
#| results: asis
if (!is.null(record$data_source)) {
  cat("**Source coverage:** ", record$data_source$cpue_scope,
      ". **Annual catch:** ", record$data_source$catch_scope, ".\n\n", sep = "")
  cat("The selected Fiji fleet index is not a validated stock-wide abundance index. ",
      "Using it with stock-wide catches is a teaching assumption.\n\n", sep = "")
} else {
  cat("The supplied index and catches define this teaching example; ",
      "inspect the producing records for their data scope.\n\n", sep = "")
}
```

The analysed series runs from `r result$series[[1]][[1]]$year` to
`r tail(result$series[[1]], 1)[[1]]$year`. Four cases combine indices A and B with fixed intrinsic growth rates of
`r paste(sort(unique(vapply(result$diagnostics, function(x) x$r, numeric(1)))), collapse = " and ")`
per year. Biomass is the start-of-year, pre-catch state. The annual Schaefer model starts it
at carrying capacity, adds surplus production and subtracts recorded catch in tonnes.
R/RTMB fits carrying capacity by minimising squared log-index residuals within
feasible bounds; log catchability is the mean difference between log index and log biomass. Initial
biomass and growth are assumptions, rather than additional fitted parameters.

## Analysis record

Produced in **`r record$run_id`**. Retained inputs keep their original producing runs.

```{r}
#| results: asis
if (!is.null(record$execution$provider)) {
  cat("Execution: ", record$execution$provider, ".\n\n", sep = "")
}
if (knitr::is_html_output()) {
  cat("Calculation image: `", record$software$container, "`\n\n", sep = "")
} else {
  digest <- sub(".*@sha256:", "", record$software$container)
  cat("Calculation image: `sha256:", substr(digest, 1, 12), "…` (full digest in the run bundle).\n\n", sep = "")
}
if (!is.null(record$software$container_url)) {
  cat("[Container package](", record$software$container_url, ")\n\n", sep = "")
}
for (key in c("source", "analysis_source")) {
  source <- record[[key]]
  if (!is.null(source$repository) && !is.null(source$commit)) {
    cat("[", key, " at ", substr(source$commit, 1, 8), "](",
        source$repository, "/commit/", source$commit, ")\n\n", sep = "")
  }
}
```

Annual values are available in the **Data** tab. Input files, settings, source versions and checksums are available in **Inputs & versions** and the downloaded run. Rendering reads saved results and does not fit models again.

# Compare fixed-growth sensitivity cases without refitting them.
calculate <- function(context) {
  keys <- c("assessment_a1", "assessment_a2", "assessment_b1", "assessment_b2")
  compare_series(context$parents[keys],
                 c("Assessment A1", "Assessment A2", "Assessment B1", "Assessment B2"),
                 assessment = TRUE)
}

Recorded component revision: 6ae03144434e26a8e70a403f4384bf8251b4eb8f
SHA-256: e1072e9ffde45764666ff932fe160b9daf505908f96aa18b8abff73d8e151f3f

# CPUE from public aggregate records, with the original synthetic example retained.

describe_data <- function(sets, catches) {
  check_sets(sets)
  check_catches(catches)
  public <- !is.null(sets[[1]]$stratum)
  group_field <- if (public) "stratum" else "vessel"
  groups <- sort(unique(rows_values(sets, group_field)))
  years <- sort(unique(rows_values(sets, "year")))
  annual <- lapply(years, function(year) {
    rows <- Filter(function(row) row$year == year, sets)
    position <- match(year, rows_values(catches, "year"))
    require_condition(!is.na(position), "A fishing year has no matching annual catch.")
    counts <- setNames(lapply(groups, function(group) {
      sum(rows_values(rows, group_field) == group)
    }), groups)
    result <- list(year = year, observations = length(rows),
         hooks = sum(rows_values(rows, "hooks")),
         catch_n = sum(rows_values(rows, "catch_n")), catch_t = catches[[position]]$catch_t,
         zero_catch_percent = 100 * mean(rows_values(rows, "catch_n") == 0))
    result[[if (public) "strata" else "vessels"]] <- counts
    if (public) {
      fleets <- sort(unique(rows_values(sets, "fleet")))
      result$fleets <- setNames(lapply(fleets, function(fleet) {
        sum(rows_values(rows, "fleet") == fleet)
      }), fleets)
    }
    result
  })
  fields <- list(
    list(name = "set_id", meaning = if (public) {
      "Unique aggregate source-row identifier (legacy field name; not a fishing set)"
    } else "Unique synthetic fishing observation", type = "text"),
    list(name = "year", meaning = "Year of fishing", type = "integer"),
    list(name = "hooks", meaning = "Fishing effort in hooks", type = "number"),
    list(name = "catch_n", meaning = "Catch in number of fish", type = "integer"))
  if (public) {
    fields <- c(fields, list(
      list(name = "fleet", meaning = "Reported fleet code", type = "text"),
      list(name = "quarter", meaning = "Calendar quarter", type = "integer"),
      list(name = "cell", meaning = "Reported five-degree spatial cell", type = "text"),
      list(name = "stratum", meaning = "Spatial cell and quarter", type = "text")))
  } else {
    fields <- c(fields, list(
      list(name = "vessel", meaning = "Synthetic vessel identifier", type = "text")))
  }
  result <- list(annual = annual, groups = json_array(groups), grouping_field = group_field,
                 observations = length(sets), fields = fields,
                 data_kind = if (public) "public-wcpfc" else "synthetic",
                 record_unit = if (public) "Public fleet–cell–quarter aggregate record" else
                   "Synthetic fishing observation")
  result[[if (public) "strata" else "vessels"]] <- json_array(groups)
  if (public) result$fleets <- json_array(sort(unique(rows_values(sets, "fleet"))))
  result
}

cpue_public <- function(sets, stratum_effect = TRUE, min_hooks = 0) {
  check_sets(sets)
  require_condition(length(unique(rows_values(sets, "fleet"))) == 1L,
                    "This aggregate CPUE model requires one selected fleet.")
  require_condition(is.logical(stratum_effect) && length(stratum_effect) == 1L &&
                      !is.na(stratum_effect), "Stratum adjustment must be TRUE or FALSE.")
  require_condition(is.numeric(min_hooks) && length(min_hooks) == 1L &&
                      is.finite(min_hooks) && min_hooks >= 0,
                    "The effort filter must be one finite non-negative number.")
  years <- sort(unique(rows_values(sets, "year")))
  strata <- sort(unique(rows_values(sets, "stratum")))
  rows <- Filter(function(row) row$hooks >= min_hooks, sets)
  check_sets(rows)
  require_condition(all(years %in% rows_values(rows, "year")) &&
                      all(strata %in% rows_values(rows, "stratum")),
                    "The effort filter removed a year or stratum from the fixed prediction support.")
  data <- data.frame(year = rows_values(rows, "year"),
                     stratum = rows_values(rows, "stratum"),
                     quarter = rows_values(rows, "quarter"),
                     hooks = rows_values(rows, "hooks"), catch_n = rows_values(rows, "catch_n"))
  annual_catch <- tapply(data$catch_n, data$year, sum)
  stratum_catch <- tapply(data$catch_n, data$stratum, sum)
  require_condition(length(years) > 1L && all(annual_catch > 0) && all(stratum_catch > 0),
                    "The aggregate GLM requires at least two years and positive annual and stratum totals.")
  adjusted <- stratum_effect && length(strata) > 1L
  seasonal <- !stratum_effect
  quarter_catch <- tapply(data$catch_n, data$quarter, sum)
  if (seasonal) {
    require_condition(length(quarter_catch) > 1L && all(quarter_catch > 0),
                      "Quarter adjustment requires at least two quarters with positive catch totals.")
  }
  formula <- if (adjusted) {
    catch_n ~ factor(year) + factor(stratum) + offset(log(hooks / 1000))
  } else if (seasonal) {
    catch_n ~ factor(year) + factor(quarter) + offset(log(hooks / 1000))
  } else {
    catch_n ~ factor(year) + offset(log(hooks / 1000))
  }
  design <- stats::model.matrix(formula, data)
  rank <- qr(design)$rank
  require_condition(rank == ncol(design) && nrow(design) > rank,
                    "The aggregate CPUE model needs a full-rank design and residual degrees of freedom.")
  fit <- stats::glm(formula, data = data, family = stats::quasipoisson(link = "log"),
                    control = stats::glm.control(epsilon = 1e-10, maxit = 100))
  require_condition(fit$converged && all(is.finite(stats::coef(fit))),
                    "The aggregate CPUE GLM did not converge to finite coefficients.")
  # The same strata and equal weights are used in every year's response-scale mean.
  grid <- expand.grid(year = years, stratum = strata, stringsAsFactors = FALSE)
  if (seasonal) {
    support <- unique(data[c("stratum", "quarter")])
    grid$quarter <- support$quarter[match(grid$stratum, support$stratum)]
  }
  grid$hooks <- 1000
  grid$weight <- 1 / length(strata)
  grid$predicted_cpue <- as.numeric(stats::predict(fit, newdata = grid, type = "response"))
  predicted <- vapply(years, function(year) {
    selected <- grid$year == year
    sum(grid$weight[selected] * grid$predicted_cpue[selected])
  }, numeric(1))
  nominal <- vapply(years, function(year) {
    selected <- data$year == year
    1000 * sum(data$catch_n[selected]) / sum(data$hooks[selected])
  }, numeric(1))
  index <- predicted / predicted[1]
  year_score <- max(abs(tapply(stats::fitted(fit), data$year, sum) / annual_catch - 1))
  stratum_score <- if (adjusted) {
    max(abs(tapply(stats::fitted(fit), data$stratum, sum) / stratum_catch - 1))
  } else 0
  quarter_score <- if (seasonal) {
    max(abs(tapply(stats::fitted(fit), data$quarter, sum) / quarter_catch - 1))
  } else 0
  residual <- max(year_score, stratum_score, quarter_score)
  dispersion <- sum(stats::residuals(fit, type = "pearson")^2) / fit$df.residual
  require_condition(all(is.finite(index) & index > 0) && residual < 1e-7 &&
                      is.finite(dispersion) && dispersion >= 0,
                    "The aggregate CPUE prediction, score or dispersion check failed.")
  result <- list(series = lapply(seq_along(years), function(i) {
         list(year = years[i], index = index[i], standardised_cpue = predicted[i],
              nominal_cpue = nominal[i])
       }),
       sets_used = length(rows), sets_excluded = length(sets) - length(rows),
       rows_included = length(rows), rows_excluded = length(sets) - length(rows),
       released_zero_rows = sum(data$catch_n == 0), min_hooks = min_hooks,
       data_kind = "public-wcpfc", record_unit = "Public fleet–cell–quarter aggregate record",
       method = if (adjusted) "Quasipoisson GLM: year + cell–quarter stratum" else
         if (seasonal) "Quasipoisson GLM: year + quarter" else "Quasipoisson GLM: year only",
       formula = paste(deparse(formula, width.cutoff = 500), collapse = " "),
       iterations = fit$iter, converged = fit$converged, deviance = fit$deviance,
       dispersion = dispersion, dispersion_method = "Pearson sum of squares / residual degrees of freedom",
       rank = rank, model_columns = ncol(design), residual_degrees_of_freedom = fit$df.residual,
       score_residual = residual, year_score_residual = year_score,
       stratum_score_residual = if (adjusted) stratum_score else NULL,
       effort_unit = "Fish per 1000 hooks", prediction_effort_hooks = 1000,
       prediction_grid = lapply(seq_len(nrow(grid)), function(i) as.list(grid[i, ])),
       weights = lapply(strata, function(stratum) list(stratum = stratum, weight = 1 / length(strata))),
       standardisation = "Arithmetic mean of response-scale predictions with equal fixed stratum weights",
       normalisation = list(reference_year = years[1], divisor = predicted[1], rule = "First year = 1"),
       limitations = json_array(c(
         "Public records aggregate fleet, spatial cell and quarter; vessel identifiers are unavailable.",
         "The index covers the selected fleet and fixed released strata, not the whole stock.",
         "Released zero counts are retained; absent or suppressed source values are not zero catch.",
         "An effort filter can require additive-model predictions for omitted year–stratum cells.",
         "This demonstration supplies no confidence intervals or official assessment advice.")))
  if (seasonal) {
    result$quarter_score_residual <- quarter_score
    first_grid <- grid[grid$year == years[1], ]
    quarter_weights <- tapply(first_grid$weight, first_grid$quarter, sum)
    result$quarter_weights <- lapply(names(quarter_weights), function(quarter) {
      list(quarter = as.integer(quarter), weight = unname(quarter_weights[quarter]))
    })
    result$limitations <- c(result$limitations, list(
      "B adjusts for quarter only; quarter effects partly confound spatial differences on this support."))
  }
  result
}

cpue <- function(sets, vessel_effect = TRUE, min_hooks = 0, stratum_effect = TRUE) {
  check_sets(sets)
  if (!is.null(sets[[1]]$stratum)) return(cpue_public(sets, stratum_effect, min_hooks))
  rows <- Filter(function(row) row$hooks >= min_hooks, sets)
  check_sets(rows)
  years <- sort(unique(rows_values(rows, "year")))
  vessels <- sort(unique(rows_values(rows, "vessel")))
  data <- data.frame(year = factor(rows_values(rows, "year"), levels = years),
                     vessel = factor(rows_values(rows, "vessel"), levels = vessels),
                     hooks = rows_values(rows, "hooks"),
                     catch_n = rows_values(rows, "catch_n"))
  require_condition(all(tapply(data$catch_n, data$year, sum) > 0) &&
                      all(tapply(data$catch_n, data$vessel, sum) > 0),
                    "The illustrative GLM requires positive annual and vessel totals.")
  formula <- if (vessel_effect && length(vessels) > 1) {
    catch_n ~ year + vessel + offset(log(hooks / 1000))
  } else {
    catch_n ~ year + offset(log(hooks / 1000))
  }
  fit <- stats::glm(formula, data = data, family = stats::poisson(),
                    control = stats::glm.control(epsilon = 1e-10, maxit = 100))
  require_condition(fit$converged, "The CPUE GLM did not converge.")
  reference <- data.frame(year = factor(years, levels = years),
                          vessel = factor(vessels[1], levels = vessels), hooks = 1000)
  predicted <- as.numeric(stats::predict(fit, newdata = reference, type = "response"))
  index <- predicted / predicted[1]
  residual <- max(abs(tapply(stats::fitted(fit), data$year, sum) /
                       tapply(data$catch_n, data$year, sum) - 1))
  require_condition(all(is.finite(index) & index > 0) && residual < 1e-7,
                    "The CPUE GLM score check failed.")
  nominal <- vapply(years, function(year) {
    selected <- data$year == year
    1000 * sum(data$catch_n[selected]) / sum(data$hooks[selected])
  }, numeric(1))
  list(series = lapply(seq_along(years), function(i) {
         list(year = years[i], index = index[i], standardised_cpue = predicted[i], nominal_cpue = nominal[i])
       }),
       sets_used = length(rows), sets_excluded = length(sets) - length(rows),
       method = if (vessel_effect) "Poisson GLM: year + vessel" else "Poisson GLM: year only",
       data_kind = "synthetic", record_unit = "Synthetic fishing observation",
       formula = paste(deparse(formula, width.cutoff = 500), collapse = " "),
       normalisation = list(reference_year = years[1], divisor = predicted[1], rule = "First year = 1"),
       standardisation = "Prediction at the first synthetic vessel and 1000 hooks",
       iterations = fit$iter, converged = fit$converged,
       deviance = fit$deviance, score_residual = residual,
       reference_vessel = vessels[1], effort_unit = "1000 hooks")
}

Recorded component revision: 6ae03144434e26a8e70a403f4384bf8251b4eb8f

# Fisheries workflow: CPUE

This repository owns 8 of the 22 demonstration jobs: submission, quality check,
database preparation, extraction, two CPUE analyses, comparison and reporting.
The coordinator loads an exact commit and preserves the source bytes used.

The public example uses WCPFC longline catch and effort records for the reported
FJ fleet. Each record combines a year, five-degree cell and quarter. Vessel
identifiers are unavailable; the legacy `set_id` field identifies an aggregate
source row. The prepared profile covers six strata in 2000–2022: the initial
snapshot ends in 2021, and the update adds 2022.

[R/cpue.R](R/cpue.R) contains the calculations. Analysis A fits a quasipoisson
GLM with year and cell–quarter effects; B fits year and quarter effects. Both use a log
link and `log(hooks / 1000)` as an effort offset. Predictions at 1000 hooks are
averaged over the same strata with equal weights in every year, then divided by
the first-year prediction. Outputs preserve nominal CPUE, standardised CPUE,
the prediction grid, weights and model checks. Quarter effects partly confound
spatial differences on this support. Released zeros are retained when
supplied. An effort filter fails if it removes an entire year or stratum.

Run [CPUE A](jobs/cpue_a/run.R) and [CPUE B](jobs/cpue_b/run.R) through the
coordinator in the declared container. These aggregate indices describe the selected fleet and
released strata. The example supplies no confidence intervals or official stock
assessment advice.

The code originated in `kyuhank/fisheries-workflow-demo` commit
`b6dc067d5346d3809edf0ad66a0f6c7e7d07ce93`; this version adds the public-data
method and retains the synthetic year/vessel Poisson example.
[SOURCE.json](SOURCE.json) records that origin and the current library checksum.

# CPUE analysis A (`cpue_a`)

Estimate annual CPUE with a quasipoisson GLM adjusting for year and cell–quarter stratum.

**Path:** Catch and effort rows → adjusted GLM → annual CPUE index A.

**Inputs:** The aggregate `sets` rows in `runs/extract/output.json`.

**From:** [Extract data (`extract`)](../extract/README.md).

**Settings:** `min_hooks_a`, recorded as `min_hooks` (default: 0).

Use a log(hooks / 1000) offset. Predict each supported stratum at 1,000 hooks and average on the response scale with equal fixed weights; divide by the first-year mean. The existing 1,200-hook filter excludes no public rows and is hidden in the public-data app. Synthetic fixtures retain the vessel-effect Poisson example.

From the repository root, run this job and prepare any required inputs:

```sh
make job JOB=cpue_a
```

[run.R](run.R) is the entry point. It receives the coordinator’s named
`context` and uses the recorded shared libraries; a copied script is not standalone.
See the [job guide](../README.md#run-and-inspect-a-job) for container and output options.

| Saved file | Contents |
| --- | --- |
| `runs/cpue_a/output.json` | annual indices, absolute CPUE, prediction support and model checks |
| `runs/cpue_a/report.html` | result page from [shared renderer](../../workflow/reports.py) |
| `runs/cpue_a/record.json` | producing inputs, settings, exact code and software |

**Used by:** [Compare CPUE results (`cpue_summary`)](../cpue_summary/README.md), [Prepare inputs A (`prepare_a`)](../prepare_a/README.md).

[Saved report](https://kyuhank.github.io/fisheries-workflow-demo/example/cpue_a/report.html) · [Run record](https://kyuhank.github.io/fisheries-workflow-demo/example/cpue_a/record.json)

# Public aggregate data: year and cell–quarter effects with an effort offset.
# Synthetic inputs retain the original year/vessel Poisson example.
calculate <- function(context) {
  cpue(context$parents$extract$sets, vessel_effect = TRUE, stratum_effect = TRUE,
       min_hooks = context$settings$min_hooks_a)
}

# CPUE analysis B (`cpue_b`)

Estimate the alternative annual CPUE index with a year-and-quarter quasipoisson GLM.

**Path:** Catch and effort rows → year-and-quarter GLM → annual CPUE index B.

**Inputs:** The aggregate `sets` rows in `runs/extract/output.json`.

**From:** [Extract data (`extract`)](../extract/README.md).

**Settings:** No job-specific settings (`{}`).

Use the same log(hooks / 1000) offset, prediction support and first-year normalisation as A, with quarter effects instead of cell–quarter effects. Quarter effects partly confound spatial differences on this support. Saved output includes nominal and standardised fish per 1,000 hooks. Synthetic fixtures retain the year-only Poisson example.

From the repository root, run this job and prepare any required inputs:

```sh
make job JOB=cpue_b
```

[run.R](run.R) is the entry point. It receives the coordinator’s named
`context` and uses the recorded shared libraries; a copied script is not standalone.
See the [job guide](../README.md#run-and-inspect-a-job) for container and output options.

| Saved file | Contents |
| --- | --- |
| `runs/cpue_b/output.json` | annual indices, absolute CPUE, prediction support and model checks |
| `runs/cpue_b/report.html` | result page from [shared renderer](../../workflow/reports.py) |
| `runs/cpue_b/record.json` | producing inputs, settings, exact code and software |

# Read preserved results. Report rendering performs no model fitting.
payload <- jsonlite::fromJSON("report-data.json", simplifyVector = FALSE)
result <- payload$result
record <- payload$record
public <- identical(result$data_kind, "public-wcpfc")
series <- result$series
analysis_a <- if (length(result$analyses)) result$analyses[[1]] else list()
rows_a <- series[[1]]
rows_frame <- function(rows) {
  do.call(rbind, lapply(rows, function(row) as.data.frame(row, stringsAsFactors = FALSE)))
}
has_field <- function(series, field) {
  length(series) > 0 && all(vapply(series, function(rows) {
    length(rows) > 0 && all(vapply(rows, function(row) {
      value <- row[[field]]
      is.numeric(value) && length(value) == 1L && is.finite(value)
    }, logical(1)))
  }, logical(1)))
}
absolute_cpue <- has_field(list(rows_a), "standardised_cpue") &&
                 has_field(list(rows_a), "nominal_cpue")
plot_series <- function(series, field, label, line_types = c(1, 2)) {
  years <- unlist(lapply(series, function(rows) vapply(rows, function(x) x$year, numeric(1))))
  values <- unlist(lapply(series, function(rows) vapply(rows, function(x) x[[field]], numeric(1))))
  colours <- rep(c("#1378a3", "#b56435", "#7357a8", "#328363"), length.out = length(series))
  line_types <- rep(line_types, length.out = length(series))
  symbols <- rep(c(16, 1, 17, 2), length.out = length(series))
  par(mar = c(4.2, 4.8, 2.8, 1))
  plot(range(years), range(c(0, values)), type = "n", xlab = "Year", ylab = label, las = 1)
  abline(h = axTicks(2), col = "#e4e9ee", lwd = 0.8)
  for (i in seq_along(series)) {
    lines(vapply(series[[i]], function(x) x$year, numeric(1)),
          vapply(series[[i]], function(x) x[[field]], numeric(1)),
          type = "o", col = colours[i], lty = line_types[i], lwd = 2,
          pch = symbols[i], cex = 0.65)
  }
  legend("top", inset = c(0, -0.16), names(series), col = colours,
         lty = line_types, lwd = 2, pch = symbols, bty = "n", horiz = TRUE, xpd = NA, cex = 0.9)
}

## Relative indices for analyses A and B

```{r}
plot_series(series, "index", "CPUE relative to first year")
```

Each index is divided by its own first-year prediction (first year = 1). `r if (public) "A adjusts cell–quarter composition; B adjusts quarter only on the same fixed prediction support." else "A adjusts vessel effects; B uses year effects only."` B may use different records because the optional effort filter applies to A.

## Methods and checks

```{r}
#| results: asis
if (public) {
  cat("The saved models are quasipoisson GLMs with a log link and ",
      "a `log(hooks / 1000)` offset. A adjusts for cell–quarter composition; ",
      "B adjusts quarter only; quarter effects partly confound spatial differences on this support. The source rows are fleet–cell–quarter aggregates, ",
      "not individual fishing sets.\n\n", sep = "")
} else if (length(result$analyses)) {
  cat("The saved synthetic models are Poisson GLMs with a log link and ",
      "a `log(hooks / 1000)` offset. A adjusts for vessel effects; ",
      "B has year effects only.\n\n", sep = "")
}
if (length(result$analyses)) {
  for (i in seq_along(result$analyses)) {
    x <- result$analyses[[i]]
    cat("**", LETTERS[i], ":** `", x$formula, "`\n\n", sep = "")
  }
  if (!is.null(analysis_a$min_hooks)) {
    cat("A effort threshold: **", analysis_a$min_hooks, " hooks per record** ",
        "(0 retains all eligible records).\n\n", sep = "")
  }
  checks <- lapply(seq_along(result$analyses), function(i) {
    x <- result$analyses[[i]]
    row <- list(Analysis = LETTERS[i], Rows_used = x$sets_used,
                Excluded = x$sets_excluded, Converged = x$converged,
                Score_error = format(x$score_residual, scientific = TRUE, digits = 3))
    if (public) row$Dispersion <- x$dispersion
    row
  })
  print(knitr::kable(rows_frame(checks), digits = 3,
                    col.names = gsub("_", " ", names(checks[[1]]))))
  if (!public) {
    cat("\nScore error is the maximum relative difference between fitted and observed annual catch totals.\n\n")
  }
}
if (public && length(analysis_a$weights)) {
  weights <- vapply(analysis_a$weights, function(x) {
    paste0("`", x$stratum, "` (", format(x$weight, digits = 4), ")")
  }, character(1))
  cat("\nA's fixed prediction weights: ", paste(weights, collapse = "; "), ".\n\n",
      "Score error is the maximum relative difference between fitted and observed annual, stratum or quarter catch totals. ",
      "Dispersion is the Pearson sum of squares divided by residual degrees of freedom.\n\n", sep = "")
}
```

## Interpretation and limits

```{r}
#| results: asis
if (public) {
  cat("The first plot shows the effect of holding cell–quarter composition fixed for A's selected records. ",
      "The index covers the selected fleet and released strata; vessel identifiers are unavailable. ",
      "Released zero counts are retained, while absent or suppressed values are not zero catch. ",
      "An effort filter may require model predictions for omitted year–stratum combinations. ",
      "No confidence intervals are supplied, and this workflow example provides no stock-wide or official assessment advice.\n\n", sep = "")
} else {
  cat("The comparison illustrates vessel adjustment for the selected synthetic observations. ",
      "No confidence intervals or fisheries advice are supplied.\n\n", sep = "")
}
```

calculate <- function(context) {

Recorded component revision: 6ae03144434e26a8e70a403f4384bf8251b4eb8f

# Correct invalid synthetic effort from the raw supplied rows, if required.
calculate <- function(context) {
  submission <- context$parents$submission
  invalid <- Filter(function(row) row$hooks <= 0 || row$catch_n < 0, submission$sets)
  returned <- json_array(rows_values(invalid, "set_id"))
  if (length(invalid) > 0) {
    submission <- source_rows(context$source, context$settings$last_year)
  }
  check_sets(submission$sets)
  public <- !is.null(submission$sets[[1]]$stratum)
  result <- list(returned = returned, rows = length(submission$sets), checks = list(
    list(check = "Positive effort", result = "Pass"),
    list(check = "Non-negative released catch", result = "Pass"),
    list(check = if (public) "Unique aggregate source-row IDs" else "Unique observation IDs", result = "Pass")))
  if (length(invalid) > 0) {
    attr(result, "workflow_effects") <- list(resubmit_submission = submission)
  }
  result
}

components/mse/R/mse.R

Recorded component revision: af0aac308321d7111b5c49b84e00a00188a5e769
SHA-256: da8b6531e0a89950c49617e2a7b6dd485e698bab7bdef06e64632a700ef7c956

# Small closed-loop trials around the fitted Schaefer cases.
# Common recorded growth/observation errors let the three rules face the same trials.

MSE_CASES <- c("assessment_a1", "assessment_a2", "assessment_b1", "assessment_b2")
MSE_RULES <- list(
  constant = list(name = "Constant catch", description = "Keep catch at its recent mean."),
  index = list(name = "Index rule", description = "Scale catch by the three-year mean observed index."),
  buffered = list(name = "Buffered rule", description = "Apply an index step, catch buffer and annual advice limit."))
MSE_ASSUMPTIONS <- list(
  seed = 20260915L, years = 15L, replicates = 20L,
  growth_cv = 0.20, observation_cv = 0.15, index_window = 3L,
  maximum_catch_multiple = 2, annual_change_limit = 0.15,
  maximum_harvest_rate = 0.4, maximum_growth_rate = 1,
  depletion_threshold = 0.2,
  buffered_steps = list(thresholds = json_array(c(0.8, 1.1)), multipliers = json_array(c(0.5, 1, 1.25))),
  rng = list(uniform = "Mersenne-Twister", normal = "Inversion", sample = "Rejection"),
  example_trial = list(case = "assessment_a1", scenario = "lower", replicate = 1L),
  scenarios = list(
    list(key = "baseline", name = "Baseline growth", growth_multipliers = json_array(rep(1, 15))),
    list(key = "lower", name = "Growth dip and recovery",
         growth_multipliers = json_array(c(rep(0.7, 6), rep(1, 9))))))

mse_prepare <- function(assessments) {
  require_condition(setequal(names(assessments), MSE_CASES),
                    "MSE preparation requires all four assessment cases.")
  models <- lapply(MSE_CASES, function(key) {
    fit <- assessments[[key]]
    rows <- fit$series
    require_condition(length(rows) >= 3 && all(diff(rows_values(rows, "year")) == 1),
                      "MSE preparation requires consecutive annual assessment rows.")
    require_condition(all(is.finite(c(fit$K, fit$r, fit$q)) & c(fit$K, fit$r, fit$q) > 0),
                      "Operating-model capacity, growth and catchability must be positive and finite.")
    catches <- rows_values(rows, "catch_t")
    path <- surplus_path(fit$K, fit$r, catches)
    # The last assessment catch is applied once by this path; start at its final state.
    B <- tail(path, 1)
    require_condition(is.finite(B) && B > 0 && B <= fit$K * (1 + 1e-8),
                      "The assessment has no feasible next-year stock state.")
    require_condition(abs(B - fit$next_biomass_t) <= 1e-8 * max(1, B),
                      "The assessment and reconstructed next-year stock do not agree.")
    recent <- tail(rows_values(rows, "observed_index"), 3)
    require_condition(all(is.finite(recent) & recent > 0),
                      "Reference CPUE indices must be positive and finite.")
    list(case = key, name = paste("Assessment", toupper(sub("assessment_", "", key))),
         first_year = tail(rows_values(rows, "year"), 1) + 1,
         B = unname(B), K = fit$K, r = fit$r, q = fit$q,
         reference_catch_t = mean(tail(catches, 3)), reference_index = mean(recent),
         recent_indices = json_array(recent), boundary_fit = fit$boundary_fit)
  })
  require_condition(length(unique(rows_values(models, "first_year"))) == 1,
                    "MSE cases must end in the same assessment year.")
  assumptions <- MSE_ASSUMPTIONS
  RNGkind(assumptions$rng$uniform, assumptions$rng$normal, assumptions$rng$sample)
  growth_sigma <- sqrt(log1p(assumptions$growth_cv^2))
  observation_sigma <- sqrt(log1p(assumptions$observation_cv^2))
  streams <- list()
  for (case in seq_along(models)) {
    for (scenario in seq_along(assumptions$scenarios)) {
      for (replicate in seq_len(assumptions$replicates)) {
        seed <- assumptions$seed + (case - 1L) * 10000L +
          (scenario - 1L) * 1000L + replicate - 1L
        set.seed(seed)
        z <- matrix(stats::rnorm(2L * assumptions$years), ncol = 2L, byrow = TRUE)
        errors <- lapply(seq_len(assumptions$years), function(year) {
          list(growth = exp(growth_sigma * z[year, 1] - growth_sigma^2 / 2),
               observation = exp(observation_sigma * z[year, 2] - observation_sigma^2 / 2))
        })
        streams[[length(streams) + 1L]] <- list(
          case = models[[case]]$case, scenario = assumptions$scenarios[[scenario]]$key,
          replicate = replicate, seed = seed,
          random_stream = paste("R/Mersenne-Twister/Inversion", seed, sep = ":"),
          errors = errors)
      }
    }
  }
  list(operating_models = models, assumptions = assumptions, rules = MSE_RULES,
       error_streams = streams,
       objectives = json_array(c("Compare catches", "Avoid low biomass", "Limit catch changes")),
       scope = "Illustrative seeded closed-loop Schaefer trials; no management advice.",
       limitations = json_array(c(
         "Fixed growth sensitivities and initial biomass; fitted cases are not weighted probabilities.",
         "Growth and observation errors are declared toy lognormal perturbations.",
         "No assessment is refitted during these management trials.",
         "Empirical trial fractions are conditional illustrations, not validated risk estimates.",
         "Annual growth is capped at one to retain the simple Euler update within its stable range.")))
}

catch_decision <- function(rule, recent, reference_index, reference_catch, previous, assumptions) {
  ratio <- mean(tail(recent, assumptions$index_window)) / reference_index
  target <- reference_catch * min(assumptions$maximum_catch_multiple, max(0, ratio))
  band <- "proportional"
  if (rule == "constant") {
    target <- reference_catch
    band <- "constant"
  }
  if (rule == "buffered") {
    steps <- assumptions$buffered_steps
    position <- sum(ratio >= numeric_values(steps$thresholds)) + 1L
    target <- reference_catch * numeric_values(steps$multipliers)[position] * assumptions$buffer
    band <- c("low", "middle", "high")[position]
    change <- assumptions$annual_change_limit
    requested <- min(previous * (1 + change), max(previous * (1 - change), target))
  } else {
    requested <- target
  }
  list(index_ratio = ratio, target_catch_t = target,
       requested_catch_t = requested, decision_band = band)
}

mse_trial <- function(model, rule, scenario, assumptions, errors) {
  B <- model$B
  recent <- numeric_values(model$recent_indices)
  previous <- model$reference_catch_t
  profile <- numeric_values(scenario$growth_multipliers)
  require_condition(length(errors) == assumptions$years && length(profile) == assumptions$years,
                    "Each MSE trial needs a growth scenario and paired errors for every year.")
  rows <- vector("list", assumptions$years)
  for (year in seq_len(assumptions$years)) {
    observed <- model$q * B * errors[[year]]$observation
    recent <- c(recent, observed)
    decision <- catch_decision(rule, recent, model$reference_index,
                               model$reference_catch_t, previous, assumptions)
    requested <- decision$requested_catch_t
    realised <- min(requested, assumptions$maximum_harvest_rate * B)
    growth <- min(assumptions$maximum_growth_rate,
                  model$r * profile[year] * errors[[year]]$growth)
    following <- B + growth * B * (1 - B / model$K) - realised
    rows[[year]] <- c(list(year = model$first_year + year - 1L, observed_index = observed), decision,
             list(catch_t = realised, harvest_rate = realised / B, biomass_t = B,
                  start_B_over_K = B / model$K, B_over_K = following / model$K,
                  growth_multiplier = profile[year], growth_rate = growth,
                  catch_shortfall_t = requested - realised))
    B <- following
    previous <- requested
  }
  catches <- c(model$reference_catch_t, rows_values(rows, "catch_t"))
  list(rows = rows, mean_catch_t = mean(catches[-1]),
       final_B_over_K = tail(rows_values(rows, "B_over_K"), 1),
       below_threshold = any(pmin(rows_values(rows, "start_B_over_K"),
                                  rows_values(rows, "B_over_K")) < assumptions$depletion_threshold),
       catch_change_percent = 100 * sum(abs(diff(catches))) / max(sum(catches[-1]), 1e-12),
       shortfall_years = sum(rows_values(rows, "catch_shortfall_t") > 1e-8))
}

mse_metrics <- function(trials) {
  list(trials = length(trials), mean_catch_t = mean(rows_values(trials, "mean_catch_t")),
       final_B_over_K = stats::median(rows_values(trials, "final_B_over_K")),
       below_threshold_percent = 100 * mean(rows_values(trials, "below_threshold")),
       catch_change_percent = mean(rows_values(trials, "catch_change_percent")),
       shortfall_trials = sum(rows_values(trials, "shortfall_years") > 0))
}

mse_annual <- function(paths) {
  fields <- c("catch_t", "B_over_K", "start_B_over_K", "index_ratio",
              "target_catch_t", "requested_catch_t", "growth_multiplier")
  lapply(seq_along(paths[[1]]), function(year) {
    rows <- lapply(paths, function(path) path[[year]])
    values <- setNames(lapply(fields, function(field) stats::median(rows_values(rows, field))), fields)
    c(list(year = rows[[1]]$year), values)
  })
}

mse_simulate <- function(prepared, rule, buffer = NULL) {
  require_condition(rule %in% names(MSE_RULES), "Unknown MSE management rule.")
  require_condition(is.null(buffer) || (rule == "buffered" && buffer %in% c(0.6, 0.8, 1)),
                    "Select one of the supplied Buffered rule catch fractions.")
  assumptions <- prepared$assumptions
  rule_settings <- if (rule == "buffered") list(buffer = optional_value(buffer, 0.8)) else empty_object()
  effective <- assumptions
  if (rule == "buffered") effective$buffer <- rule_settings$buffer
  trials <- list()
  paths <- list()
  example <- NULL
  for (stream in prepared$error_streams) {
    model <- prepared$operating_models[[match(stream$case, rows_values(prepared$operating_models, "case"))]]
    scenario <- assumptions$scenarios[[match(stream$scenario, rows_values(assumptions$scenarios, "key"))]]
    trial <- mse_trial(model, rule, scenario, effective, stream$errors)
    paths[[length(paths) + 1L]] <- trial$rows
    trials[[length(trials) + 1L]] <- c(trial[names(trial) != "rows"],
      stream[c("case", "scenario", "replicate", "seed", "random_stream")])
    selected <- assumptions$example_trial
    if (stream$case == selected$case && stream$scenario == selected$scenario &&
        stream$replicate == selected$replicate) {
      example <- list(case = model$name, scenario = scenario$name, case_key = stream$case,
                      scenario_key = stream$scenario, replicate = stream$replicate,
                      seed = stream$seed, rows = trial$rows,
                      reference_catch_t = model$reference_catch_t,
                      reference_index = model$reference_index,
                      selection = "Specified before simulation; the same trial is shown for every rule.")
    }
  }
  require_condition(length(trials) > 0 && !is.null(example), "The selected MSE trials are missing.")
  scenarios <- lapply(assumptions$scenarios, function(scenario) {
    selected <- which(rows_values(trials, "scenario") == scenario$key)
    list(key = scenario$key, name = scenario$name, series = mse_annual(paths[selected]),
         metrics = mse_metrics(trials[selected]))
  })
  cases <- list()
  for (model in prepared$operating_models) {
    for (scenario in assumptions$scenarios) {
      selected <- Filter(function(row) row$case == model$case && row$scenario == scenario$key, trials)
      cases[[length(cases) + 1L]] <- c(list(case = model$name, scenario = scenario$name), mse_metrics(selected))
    }
  }
  annual <- lapply(mse_annual(paths), function(row) row[c("year", "catch_t", "B_over_K")])
  description <- MSE_RULES[[rule]]$description
  if (rule == "buffered") description <- sprintf(
    "Apply an index step and %.0f%% catch buffer; limit annual advice changes to %.0f%%.",
    100 * rule_settings$buffer, 100 * assumptions$annual_change_limit)
  list(rule = rule, name = MSE_RULES[[rule]]$name, description = description,
       rule_settings = rule_settings, assumptions = assumptions,
       metrics = mse_metrics(trials), series = annual, scenarios = scenarios, cases = cases,
       trials = trials, example = example, operating_models = prepared$operating_models,
       error_streams = prepared$error_streams, limitations = prepared$limitations,
       scope = prepared$scope,
       boundary_cases = json_array(rows_values(Filter(function(model) model$boundary_fit,
                                                prepared$operating_models), "name")))
}

mse_summarise <- function(results) {
  by_rule <- setNames(results, rows_values(results, "rule"))
  require_condition(length(results) == 3 && setequal(names(by_rule), names(MSE_RULES)),
                    "MSE comparison requires each of the three management rules.")
  first <- by_rule$constant
  for (result in by_rule) {
    require_condition(identical(result$assumptions, first$assumptions) &&
                        identical(result$operating_models, first$operating_models) &&
                        identical(result$error_streams, first$error_streams),
                      "MSE rules must use the same operating models, scenarios and actual error streams.")
  }
  rule_names <- vapply(by_rule, function(result) result$name, character(1))
  metrics <- lapply(names(MSE_RULES), function(rule) {
    c(list(rule = rule, name = by_rule[[rule]]$name), by_rule[[rule]]$metrics)
  })
  cases <- unname(do.call(c, lapply(by_rule, function(result) {
    lapply(result$cases, function(row) c(list(rule = result$name), row))
  })))
  scenarios <- lapply(seq_along(first$scenarios), function(i) {
    list(key = first$scenarios[[i]]$key, name = first$scenarios[[i]]$name,
         series = setNames(lapply(by_rule, function(result) result$scenarios[[i]]$series), rule_names),
         metrics = lapply(names(MSE_RULES), function(rule) {
           c(list(rule = rule, name = by_rule[[rule]]$name), by_rule[[rule]]$scenarios[[i]]$metrics)
         }))
  })
  list(series = setNames(lapply(by_rule, function(result) result$series), rule_names), metrics = metrics,
       cases = cases, assumptions = first$assumptions,
       rules = lapply(by_rule, function(result) {
         list(name = result$name, description = result$description, settings = result$rule_settings)
       }), operating_models = first$operating_models, error_streams = first$error_streams,
       limitations = first$limitations, boundary_cases = first$boundary_cases, scope = first$scope,
       scenarios = scenarios, examples = setNames(lapply(by_rule, function(result) result$example), rule_names))
}

Recorded component revision: af0aac308321d7111b5c49b84e00a00188a5e769

# Fisheries workflow MSE example

These six jobs prepare common future trials, test constant catch, an index rule
and a buffered rule, then compare their catch, biomass and catch stability.
The [coordinator](https://github.com/kyuhank/fisheries-workflow-demo) loads an exact
source revision and preserves each producing record.

The fitted Schaefer cases are teaching inputs. In the public-data example they
inherit an unvalidated link between the selected Fiji fleet CPUE and stock-wide
abundance. The same saved growth and observation errors are used for all rules.

Realised catch is capped at 40% of true starting biomass, a simulator guardrail
shared by every rule. Low-biomass statistics are conditional on that safeguard;
they do not show that a rule would be safe without it. Cases and scenarios have
equal comparison weight, rather than estimated probabilities. The example
provides no calibrated risk estimates or real-fishery advice.

Run through the coordinator in its recorded container, for example:

```sh
make job JOB=mse_index
```

Each job receives a named `context`; its entry point is not standalone. See
`jobs/*/README.md` for input connections. The report reads saved results without
rerunning trials and keeps detailed metrics, assumptions and source records.

[SOURCE.json](SOURCE.json) records the calculation library's baseline origin and
SHA-256. The R library and calculation entry points retain those source bytes;
the report and reader guides have been revised for the public-data example.

# Buffered rule (`mse_buffered`)

Test index-based catch steps with the selected buffer and a 15% annual advice-change limit in the common seeded Schaefer management trials.

**Path:** Shared trials → buffered catch target → catch and biomass outcomes.

**Inputs:** Stocks, scenarios and saved `error_streams` in `runs/mse_prepare/output.json`.

**From:** [Prepare MSE (`mse_prepare`)](../mse_prepare/README.md).

**Settings:** `mse_buffer` saved as `buffer` (default: 0.8).

MSE must be enabled (`"mse": true`). The declared trials use 15 years, 20 paired replicates per fitted case and growth scenario, with catches capped at 40% of current biomass.

From the repository root, run this job and prepare any required inputs:

```sh
make job JOB=mse_buffered
```

[run.R](run.R) is the entry point. It receives the coordinator’s named
`context` and uses the recorded shared libraries; a copied script is not standalone.
See the [job guide](../README.md#run-and-inspect-a-job) for container and output options.

# Prepare MSE (`mse_prepare`)
