Compare catch, biomass and catch stability for three rules under the same simulated future conditions.

**Path:** Four fitted cases → shared trials and catch rules → management comparison.

```{r}
# Read saved results without fitting or simulating again.
payload <- jsonlite::fromJSON("report-data.json", simplifyVector = FALSE)
result <- payload$result
record <- payload$record
rows_frame <- function(rows) {
  do.call(rbind, lapply(rows, function(row) as.data.frame(row, stringsAsFactors = FALSE)))
}
plot_series <- function(series, field, label) {
  years <- unlist(lapply(series, function(rows) vapply(rows, function(x) x$year, numeric(1))))
  values <- unlist(lapply(series, function(rows) vapply(rows, function(x) x[[field]], numeric(1))))
  colours <- c("#1378a3", "#b56435", "#7357a8", "#328363")
  old_mar <- par("mar")
  on.exit(par(mar = old_mar))
  par(mar = c(4, 4, 5, 1))
  plot(range(years), range(c(0, values)), type = "n", xlab = "Year", ylab = label)
  for (i in seq_along(series)) {
    lines(vapply(series[[i]], function(x) x$year, numeric(1)),
          vapply(series[[i]], function(x) x[[field]], numeric(1)),
          col = colours[(i - 1) %% length(colours) + 1], lwd = 2)
  }
  legend("top", names(series), inset = c(0, -0.34), xpd = NA,
         col = colours[seq_along(series)], lty = 1, lwd = 2, cex = 0.8, bty = "n")
}
```

## Management comparison

```{r}
# Use the recorded rule order for consistent colours in every plot.
rule_names <- vapply(result$metrics, function(row) row$name, character(1))
plot_series(result$series[rule_names], "B_over_K", "Median end-year biomass / carrying capacity")
```

Compare biomass alongside realised catch and annual catch changes. These
conditional trials provide neither calibrated risk estimates nor evidence that
a rule would be safe without the shared harvest guardrail. Fixed case differences
do not include assessment parameter uncertainty or select a rule for a real fishery.

```{r}
#| results: asis
if (length(result$boundary_cases)) {
  cat("**Review needed:** assessment search boundaries were reached for ",
      paste(unlist(result$boundary_cases), collapse = ", "), ".\n\n", sep = "")
}
if (any(vapply(result$metrics, function(row) row$shortfall_trials > 0, logical(1)))) {
  cat("Some requested catches exceeded the harvest guardrail. Metrics use realised catches.\n\n")
}
```

### Catch, biomass and catch stability

```{r}
metrics <- lapply(result$metrics, function(row) {
  list(rule = row$name, mean_catch_t = row$mean_catch_t,
       final_B_over_K = row$final_B_over_K,
       below_threshold_percent = row$below_threshold_percent,
       catch_change_percent = row$catch_change_percent)
})
knitr::kable(rows_frame(metrics), digits = 3,
             col.names = c("Rule", "Mean catch (t/year)", "Final B/K",
                           "Trials below 0.2 (%)", "Catch change (%)"))
```

Mean catch is in tonnes per year; final B/K is the median across trials. The
threshold column counts trials below `r result$assumptions$depletion_threshold`
of carrying capacity at any time. Catch change averages each trial's `100 × sum(abs(C_t − C_(t−1))) / sum(C_t)` using realised catches,
including the first change from the reference catch. Cases and scenarios have equal weight, not estimated probabilities.



### Trajectories by growth scenario

```{r}
par(mfrow = c(1, 2), mar = c(4, 4, 3, 1))
for (scenario in result$scenarios) {
  series <- scenario$series[rule_names]
  label <- sub(" growth$", "", sub(" and recovery$", "", scenario$name))
  plot_series(series, "B_over_K", paste0(label, ": median B/K"))
  plot_series(series, "catch_t", paste0(label, ": median catch (t)"))
}
```



### Trial assumptions and limits

One scenario keeps the growth multiplier at one. The other uses 0.7 for six
years, then returns to one. Mean-one lognormal growth and observation errors
have coefficients of variation `r result$assumptions$growth_cv` and
`r result$assumptions$observation_cv` before the growth cap is applied. The
preparation result preserves seeds, RNG settings and actual paired error vectors.
Surplus production uses starting biomass; realised catch is then removed.

```{r}
#| results: asis
for (limitation in result$limitations) cat("- ", limitation, "\n", sep = "")
```




## Methods


```{r}
#| results: asis
if (!is.null(record$data_source)) {
  cat("**Source coverage:** ", record$data_source$cpue_scope,
      ". **Annual catch:** ", record$data_source$catch_scope, ".\n\n", sep = "")
  cat("The selected Fiji fleet index is not a validated stock-wide abundance index. ",
      "Using it with stock-wide catches is a teaching assumption.\n\n", sep = "")
} else {
  cat("The supplied index and catches define this teaching example; ",
      "inspect the producing records for their data scope.\n\n", sep = "")
}
```

Four fitted Schaefer cases supply starting biomass, capacity, growth and
catchability. Constant catch, an index rule and a buffered rule use the same
saved growth and observation errors for **`r result$assumptions$years` years**,
with **`r result$assumptions$replicates` trials** per case and scenario.
Each year, observe an index → set catch → update biomass → observe again.
The index rules use a three-year observed mean. Reference catch C₀ and index
I₀ are the last three historical-year means. Rₜ is the mean of the current
simulated index and previous two indices, divided by I₀.

| Rule | Requested catch |
|:--|:--|
| Constant | C₀ |
| Index | C₀ × min(`r result$assumptions$maximum_catch_multiple`, Rₜ) |
| Buffered | b × C₀ × m(Rₜ), limited to ±`r 100 * result$assumptions$annual_change_limit`% of the previous request (initially C₀) |

Here b is `r result$rules$buffered$settings$buffer`. The step multiplier m is
`r paste(unlist(result$assumptions$buffered_steps$multipliers), collapse = ", ")`
for Rₜ below `r result$assumptions$buffered_steps$thresholds[[1]]`, between
`r paste(unlist(result$assumptions$buffered_steps$thresholds), collapse = " and ")`,
or at least `r result$assumptions$buffered_steps$thresholds[[2]]`, respectively.

**Shared guardrail:** realised catch is capped at
`r 100 * result$assumptions$maximum_harvest_rate`% of true starting biomass.
This simulator safeguard uses biomass unavailable to the index rules; every
rule's biomass and low-biomass statistics are conditional on that guardrail.
Growth is capped at `r result$assumptions$maximum_growth_rate` per year.

## Analysis record

Produced in **`r record$run_id`**. Retained inputs keep their original producing runs.

```{r}
#| results: asis
if (!is.null(record$execution$provider)) {
  cat("Execution: ", record$execution$provider, ".\n\n", sep = "")
}
if (knitr::is_html_output()) {
  cat("Calculation image: `", record$software$container, "`\n\n", sep = "")
} else {
  digest <- sub(".*@sha256:", "", record$software$container)
  cat("Calculation image: `sha256:", substr(digest, 1, 12), "…` (full digest in the run bundle).\n\n", sep = "")
}
if (!is.null(record$software$container_url)) {
  cat("[Container package](", record$software$container_url, ")\n\n", sep = "")
}
for (key in c("source", "analysis_source")) {
  source <- record[[key]]
  if (!is.null(source$repository) && !is.null(source$commit)) {
    cat("[", key, " at ", substr(source$commit, 1, 8), "](",
        source$repository, "/commit/", source$commit, ")\n\n", sep = "")
  }
}
```

Annual values are available in the **Data** tab. Input files, settings, source versions and checksums are available in **Inputs & versions** and the downloaded run. Rendering reads saved results and does not fit models again.

# MSE results summary (`mse_summary`)

Compare the three rules only when fitted stocks, scenarios and the actual saved error vectors match. Summarise catches, biomass and catch stability.

**Path:** Three rule outputs → compare trial metrics → management comparison.

**Inputs:** Completed constant, index and buffered rule outputs, including their actual common error vectors.

**From:** [Constant catch (`mse_constant`)](../mse_constant/README.md), [Index rule (`mse_index`)](../mse_index/README.md), [Buffered rule (`mse_buffered`)](../mse_buffered/README.md).

**Settings:** No job-specific settings (`{}`).

MSE must be enabled (`"mse": true`). The declared trials use 15 years, 20 paired replicates per fitted case and growth scenario, with catches capped at 40% of current biomass.

From the repository root, run this job and prepare any required inputs:

```sh
make job JOB=mse_summary
```

[run.R](run.R) is the entry point. It receives the coordinator’s named
`context` and uses the recorded shared libraries; a copied script is not standalone.
See the [job guide](../README.md#run-and-inspect-a-job) for container and output options.

| --- | --- |
| `runs/mse_summary/output.json` | saved calculation result |
| `runs/mse_summary/report.html` | result page from [shared renderer](../../workflow/reports.py) |
| `runs/mse_summary/record.json` | producing inputs, settings, exact code and software |

jobs/README.md

# Jobs and their connections

The 22 jobs pass public catch and effort data through CPUE, assessment and MSE.
Each guide states its input → method → output path. The calculations use
quasipoisson CPUE GLMs, fixed-growth RTMB Schaefer fits and seeded management
trials. These simplified models provide no real-stock advice.

| Job | Required producing jobs |
| --- | --- |
| [Data submission](submission/README.md) | supplied records |
| [Quality check](qc/README.md) | `submission` |
| [Prepare and load](database/README.md) | `qc` |
| [Extract data](extract/README.md) | `database` |
| [CPUE analysis A](cpue_a/README.md) | `extract` |
| [CPUE analysis B](cpue_b/README.md) | `extract` |
| [Compare CPUE results](cpue_summary/README.md) | `cpue_a`, `cpue_b` |
| [CPUE report](cpue_report/README.md) | `cpue_summary` |
| [Prepare inputs A](prepare_a/README.md) | `extract`, `cpue_a` |
| [Prepare inputs B](prepare_b/README.md) | `extract`, `cpue_b` |
| [Assessment A1](assessment_a1/README.md) | `prepare_a` |
| [Assessment A2](assessment_a2/README.md) | `prepare_a` |
| [Assessment B1](assessment_b1/README.md) | `prepare_b` |
| [Assessment B2](assessment_b2/README.md) | `prepare_b` |
| [Compare assessments](assessment_summary/README.md) | `assessment_a1`, `assessment_a2`, `assessment_b1`, `assessment_b2` |
| [Assessment report](assessment_report/README.md) | `assessment_summary` |
| [Prepare MSE](mse_prepare/README.md) | `assessment_a1`, `assessment_a2`, `assessment_b1`, `assessment_b2` |
| [Constant catch](mse_constant/README.md) | `mse_prepare` |
| [Index rule](mse_index/README.md) | `mse_prepare` |
| [Buffered rule](mse_buffered/README.md) | `mse_prepare` |
| [MSE results summary](mse_summary/README.md) | `mse_constant`, `mse_index`, `mse_buffered` |
| [MSE report](mse_report/README.md) | `mse_summary` |

For **Prepare and load**, QC is the scheduling gate. The accepted submission is
the source artifact stored in SQLite; its record is retained alongside the QC result.

## Read and adapt a job

The [job declarations](../workflow/jobs.json) name the entry point and its parent
jobs. The coordinator starts a job when required inputs are ready and records
the producing run, signature and file checksum before passing its result onward.

An R entry point receives a named `context` and returns a result. For example,
[CPUE A](cpue_a/run.R) uses the shared CPUE function:

```r
calculate <- function(context) {
  cpue(context$parents$extract$sets, stratum_effect = TRUE,
       min_hooks = context$settings$min_hooks_a)
}
```

For public records, this fits year and cell–quarter effects with a log effort
offset. The saved result contains the prediction grid and weights as well as the
annual index. The same entry point retains the earlier synthetic-vessel example.
Shared libraries are loaded by the R driver; a copied `run.R` is not standalone.

To adapt a fishery, first map its source fields and units, then review the saved
SQL and CPUE prediction support. Change later assessment and management assumptions
only after checking their inputs and receiving contracts. [ADAPT.md](../ADAPT.md)
explains adding jobs, declaring connections and changing source pins.

| File | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `jobs/*/run.R` | one named calculation |
| `jobs/*_report/report.qmd` | complete analysis reports from saved results |
| `workflow/results.qmd` | concise Quarto result views; plots use saved numerical outputs |
| `workflow/r/common.R` | row checks, source selection and annual joins |
| `workflow/r/models.R` | monorepo CPUE and Schaefer functions |
| `workflow/r/mse.R` | management trials with shared random errors |
| `workflow/jobs.json` | parents, execution stages and handovers |
| `workflow/engine.py` | scheduling, saved records and reuse |

## Run and inspect a job

From the repository root, with Docker, Make, Python 3 and Git available:

```sh
make job JOB=cpue_a
```

The coordinator prepares missing or changed inputs and calls the R script in
the recorded container. `OUTPUT=DIR` selects the result directory;
`SETTINGS=FILE` supplies JSON settings. Inside the declared container, use
`make inside-job JOB=cpue_a`.

| File | Contents |
| --- | --- |
| `runs/cpue_a/output.json` | numerical result and method metadata |
| `runs/cpue_a/report.pdf` | compiled Quarto result PDF |
| `runs/cpue_a/record.json` | input producers, settings, exact code and software |
| `runs/database/snapshot.sqlite` | accepted data snapshot |
| `runs/state.json` | execution state and events |

CPUE transfers supply annual relative `index` values. Preparation joins these
to `catch_t` in tonnes for matching consecutive years. Assessment records retain
convergence, gradient, boundary, positive-biomass and annual balance checks.
Differences among the four fixed-growth cases are not confidence intervals.

MSE preparation receives positive finite growth, capacity and catchability,
the observed indices and catches, and the declared next-year biomass. The
management trials use the same seeded errors for each rule. Trial fractions are
conditional comparisons under the supplied cases and scenarios.

## Separate repositories

The source lock records exact revisions from three component repositories:

| Repository | Jobs | Calculation library |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [Preparation/CPUE](https://github.com/kyuhank/fisheries-workflow-cpue-demo) | submission through CPUE report (8) | `R/cpue.R` |
| [Assessment](https://github.com/kyuhank/fisheries-workflow-assessment-demo) | input preparation through assessment report (8) | `R/assessment.R` |
| [MSE](https://github.com/kyuhank/fisheries-workflow-mse-demo) | MSE preparation through MSE report (6) | `R/mse.R` |

The coordinator verifies the source bytes named in
[source-lock.template.json](../source-lock.template.json). Shared row and JSON
handling stays in `workflow/r/common.R`. MSE preparation also uses the declared
assessment library. Downloaded runs contain the exact component snapshots and
origin manifests; unchanged dependencies keep their producing records.
[ADAPT.md](../ADAPT.md#separate-analyst-repositories) describes source assembly and
the explicit monorepo fallback. Repository separation uses one coordinator and
does not establish collaboration by several human operators.

# Assessment A1 (`assessment_a1`)

Fit the toy Schaefer model to index A with fixed intrinsic growth r = 0.25 and initial biomass equal to K. RTMB estimates one log(K) parameter; catchability q is profiled.

**Path:** Annual index and catches → fixed-growth Schaefer fit → biomass trajectory.

**Inputs:** Joined index and catch `rows` in `runs/prepare_a/output.json`.

**From:** [Prepare inputs A (`prepare_a`)](../prepare_a/README.md).

**Settings:** Fixed `r = 0.25`.

Biomass is reported as B/K and catches in tonnes. Growth and initial depletion are fixed sensitivity assumptions; convergence does not establish real-stock validity or uncertainty.

From the repository root, run this job and prepare any required inputs:

```sh
make job JOB=assessment_a1
```

[run.R](run.R) is the entry point. It receives the coordinator’s named
`context` and uses the recorded shared libraries; a copied script is not standalone.
See the [job guide](../README.md#run-and-inspect-a-job) for container and output options.

| Saved file | Contents |
| --- | --- |
| `runs/assessment_a1/output.json` | saved calculation result |
| `runs/assessment_a1/report.html` | result page from [shared renderer](../../workflow/reports.py) |
| `runs/assessment_a1/record.json` | producing inputs, settings, exact code and software |

**Used by:** [Compare assessments (`assessment_summary`)](../assessment_summary/README.md), [Prepare MSE (`mse_prepare`)](../mse_prepare/README.md).

Compare how two CPUE indices and two fixed growth assumptions affect the fitted biomass trajectory.

**Path:** Annual index and catches → fixed-growth Schaefer fit → biomass trajectory.

```{r}
# Read saved results without fitting or simulating again.
payload <- jsonlite::fromJSON("report-data.json", simplifyVector = FALSE)
result <- payload$result
record <- payload$record
rows_frame <- function(rows) {
  do.call(rbind, lapply(rows, function(row) as.data.frame(row, stringsAsFactors = FALSE)))
}
plot_series <- function(series, field, label) {
  years <- unlist(lapply(series, function(rows) vapply(rows, function(x) x$year, numeric(1))))
  values <- unlist(lapply(series, function(rows) vapply(rows, function(x) x[[field]], numeric(1))))
  colours <- c("#1378a3", "#b56435", "#7357a8", "#328363")
  plot(range(years), range(c(0, values)), type = "n", xlab = "Year", ylab = label)
  for (i in seq_along(series)) {
    lines(vapply(series[[i]], function(x) x$year, numeric(1)),
          vapply(series[[i]], function(x) x[[field]], numeric(1)),
          col = colours[(i - 1) %% length(colours) + 1], lwd = 2)
  }
  legend("topright", names(series), col = colours[seq_along(series)], lty = 1, bty = "n")
}
```

## Biomass comparison

```{r}
plot_series(result$series, "B_over_K", "Biomass / carrying capacity")
```

The trajectories describe the supplied index, catches and fixed model assumptions.
Differences among cases are not an uncertainty interval or stock-management advice.

```{r}
#| results: asis
review <- vapply(result$diagnostics, function(row) {
  isTRUE(row$boundary_fit) || (!is.null(row$convergence) && row$convergence != 0) ||
    any(vapply(c("balance_check", "feasibility_check", "gradient_check"), function(key) {
      !is.null(row[[key]]) && row[[key]] != "Pass"
    }, logical(1)))
}, logical(1))
if (any(review)) {
  cat("**Review needed:** ", paste(vapply(result$diagnostics[review], function(row) row$case,
      character(1)), collapse = ", "), ". Inspect the recorded fit checks below.\n\n", sep = "")
}
```

### Final annual values and fit checks

```{r}
endpoints <- lapply(names(result$series), function(name) {
  row <- tail(result$series[[name]], 1)[[1]]
  list(case = name, year = row$year, B_over_K = row$B_over_K,
       harvest_rate = row$harvest_rate)
})
knitr::kable(rows_frame(endpoints), digits = 3,
             col.names = c("Case", "Year", "B/K", "Harvest rate"))
checks <- lapply(result$diagnostics, function(row) {
  list(case = row$case, intrinsic_growth = row$r,
       biomass_balance_check = row$balance_check, feasibility = row$feasibility_check,
       gradient_check = row$gradient_check,
       boundary_fit = row$boundary_fit, convergence = row$convergence)
})
knitr::kable(rows_frame(checks), digits = 7,
             col.names = c("Case", "Growth (r)", "Balance", "Feasible",
                           "Gradient", "Boundary", "Convergence"))
```

Convergence, biomass balance and positive-biomass feasibility check the fitted
calculation. They do not establish biological adequacy or validate the CPUE
as a stock-wide abundance measure.




## Methods


```{r}
#| results: asis
if (!is.null(record$data_source)) {
  cat("**Source coverage:** ", record$data_source$cpue_scope,
      ". **Annual catch:** ", record$data_source$catch_scope, ".\n\n", sep = "")
  cat("The selected Fiji fleet index is not a validated stock-wide abundance index. ",
      "Using it with stock-wide catches is a teaching assumption.\n\n", sep = "")
} else {
  cat("The supplied index and catches define this teaching example; ",
      "inspect the producing records for their data scope.\n\n", sep = "")
}
```

The analysed series runs from `r result$series[[1]][[1]]$year` to
`r tail(result$series[[1]], 1)[[1]]$year`. Four cases combine indices A and B with fixed intrinsic growth rates of
`r paste(sort(unique(vapply(result$diagnostics, function(x) x$r, numeric(1)))), collapse = " and ")`
per year. Biomass is the start-of-year, pre-catch state. The annual Schaefer model starts it
at carrying capacity, adds surplus production and subtracts recorded catch in tonnes.
R/RTMB fits carrying capacity by minimising squared log-index residuals within
feasible bounds; log catchability is the mean difference between log index and log biomass. Initial
biomass and growth are assumptions, rather than additional fitted parameters.

## Analysis record

Produced in **`r record$run_id`**. Retained inputs keep their original producing runs.

```{r}
#| results: asis
if (!is.null(record$execution$provider)) {
  cat("Execution: ", record$execution$provider, ".\n\n", sep = "")
}
if (knitr::is_html_output()) {
  cat("Calculation image: `", record$software$container, "`\n\n", sep = "")
} else {
  digest <- sub(".*@sha256:", "", record$software$container)
  cat("Calculation image: `sha256:", substr(digest, 1, 12), "…` (full digest in the run bundle).\n\n", sep = "")
}
if (!is.null(record$software$container_url)) {
  cat("[Container package](", record$software$container_url, ")\n\n", sep = "")
}
for (key in c("source", "analysis_source")) {
  source <- record[[key]]
  if (!is.null(source$repository) && !is.null(source$commit)) {
    cat("[", key, " at ", substr(source$commit, 1, 8), "](",
        source$repository, "/commit/", source$commit, ")\n\n", sep = "")
  }
}
```

Annual values are available in the **Data** tab. Input files, settings, source versions and checksums are available in **Inputs & versions** and the downloaded run. Rendering reads saved results and does not fit models again.

# Compare assessments (`assessment_summary`)

Compare the four fixed-growth Schaefer sensitivity cases, their biomass trajectories and fit diagnostics.

**Path:** Four fitted cases → compare trajectories → sensitivity summary.

**Inputs:** The fitted `series`, r, K, convergence and bounds in all four assessment outputs.

**From:** [Assessment A1 (`assessment_a1`)](../assessment_a1/README.md), [Assessment A2 (`assessment_a2`)](../assessment_a2/README.md), [Assessment B1 (`assessment_b1`)](../assessment_b1/README.md), [Assessment B2 (`assessment_b2`)](../assessment_b2/README.md).

**Settings:** No job-specific settings (`{}`).

Biomass is reported as B/K and catches in tonnes. Growth and initial depletion are fixed sensitivity assumptions; convergence does not establish real-stock validity or uncertainty.

From the repository root, run this job and prepare any required inputs:

# CPUE analysis A (`cpue_a`)

Estimate annual CPUE with a quasipoisson GLM adjusting for year and cell–quarter stratum.

**Path:** Catch and effort rows → adjusted GLM → annual CPUE index A.

**Inputs:** The aggregate `sets` rows in `runs/extract/output.json`.

**From:** [Extract data (`extract`)](../extract/README.md).

**Settings:** `min_hooks_a`, recorded as `min_hooks` (default: 0).

Use a log(hooks / 1000) offset. Predict each supported stratum at 1,000 hooks and average on the response scale with equal fixed weights; divide by the first-year mean. The existing 1,200-hook filter excludes no public rows and is hidden in the public-data app. Synthetic fixtures retain the vessel-effect Poisson example.

From the repository root, run this job and prepare any required inputs:

```sh
make job JOB=cpue_a
```

[run.R](run.R) is the entry point. It receives the coordinator’s named
`context` and uses the recorded shared libraries; a copied script is not standalone.
See the [job guide](../README.md#run-and-inspect-a-job) for container and output options.

| Saved file | Contents |
| --- | --- |
| `runs/cpue_a/output.json` | annual indices, absolute CPUE, prediction support and model checks |
| `runs/cpue_a/report.html` | result page from [shared renderer](../../workflow/reports.py) |
| `runs/cpue_a/record.json` | producing inputs, settings, exact code and software |

**Used by:** [Compare CPUE results (`cpue_summary`)](../cpue_summary/README.md), [Prepare inputs A (`prepare_a`)](../prepare_a/README.md).

[Saved report](https://kyuhank.github.io/fisheries-workflow-demo/example/cpue_a/report.html) · [Run record](https://kyuhank.github.io/fisheries-workflow-demo/example/cpue_a/record.json)

# CPUE analysis B (`cpue_b`)

Estimate the alternative annual CPUE index with a year-and-quarter quasipoisson GLM.

**Path:** Catch and effort rows → year-and-quarter GLM → annual CPUE index B.

**Inputs:** The aggregate `sets` rows in `runs/extract/output.json`.

**From:** [Extract data (`extract`)](../extract/README.md).

**Settings:** No job-specific settings (`{}`).

Use the same log(hooks / 1000) offset, prediction support and first-year normalisation as A, with quarter effects instead of cell–quarter effects. Quarter effects partly confound spatial differences on this support. Saved output includes nominal and standardised fish per 1,000 hooks. Synthetic fixtures retain the year-only Poisson example.

See how standardisation changes annual CPUE, then compare the two saved relative indices.

```{r}
# Read preserved results. Report rendering performs no model fitting.
payload <- jsonlite::fromJSON("report-data.json", simplifyVector = FALSE)
result <- payload$result
record <- payload$record
public <- identical(result$data_kind, "public-wcpfc")
series <- result$series
analysis_a <- if (length(result$analyses)) result$analyses[[1]] else list()
rows_a <- series[[1]]
rows_frame <- function(rows) {
  do.call(rbind, lapply(rows, function(row) as.data.frame(row, stringsAsFactors = FALSE)))
}
has_field <- function(series, field) {
  length(series) > 0 && all(vapply(series, function(rows) {
    length(rows) > 0 && all(vapply(rows, function(row) {
      value <- row[[field]]
      is.numeric(value) && length(value) == 1L && is.finite(value)
    }, logical(1)))
  }, logical(1)))
}
absolute_cpue <- has_field(list(rows_a), "standardised_cpue") &&
                 has_field(list(rows_a), "nominal_cpue")
plot_series <- function(series, field, label, line_types = c(1, 2)) {
  years <- unlist(lapply(series, function(rows) vapply(rows, function(x) x$year, numeric(1))))
  values <- unlist(lapply(series, function(rows) vapply(rows, function(x) x[[field]], numeric(1))))
  colours <- rep(c("#1378a3", "#b56435", "#7357a8", "#328363"), length.out = length(series))
  line_types <- rep(line_types, length.out = length(series))
  symbols <- rep(c(16, 1, 17, 2), length.out = length(series))
  par(mar = c(4.2, 4.8, 2.8, 1))
  plot(range(years), range(c(0, values)), type = "n", xlab = "Year", ylab = label, las = 1)
  abline(h = axTicks(2), col = "#e4e9ee", lwd = 0.8)
  for (i in seq_along(series)) {
    lines(vapply(series[[i]], function(x) x$year, numeric(1)),
          vapply(series[[i]], function(x) x[[field]], numeric(1)),
          type = "o", col = colours[i], lty = line_types[i], lwd = 2,
          pch = symbols[i], cex = 0.65)
  }
  legend("top", inset = c(0, -0.16), names(series), col = colours,
         lty = line_types, lwd = 2, pch = symbols, bty = "n", horiz = TRUE, xpd = NA, cex = 0.9)
}
```

## Standardised and nominal CPUE

```{r}
if (absolute_cpue) {
  nominal_a <- lapply(rows_a, function(row) {
    list(year = row$year, standardised_cpue = row$nominal_cpue)
  })
  plot_series(list("Standardised A" = rows_a, "Nominal: A records" = nominal_a),
              "standardised_cpue", "Fish per 1,000 hooks")
}
```

```{r}
#| results: asis
if (!absolute_cpue) {
  cat("This saved result contains relative indices only; an absolute standardised–nominal comparison is unavailable.\n\n")
} else if (public) {
  cat("Standardised A averages predictions at 1,000 hooks over the same ",
      length(analysis_a$weights), " cell–quarter strata with equal weights each year. ",
      "Nominal CPUE is 1,000 × annual total catch / annual total hooks from the same A records. ",
      "Any A effort filter applies to both curves.\n\n", sep = "")
} else {
  cat("Standardised A predicts at 1,000 hooks for the first synthetic vessel. ",
      "Nominal CPUE is 1,000 × annual total catch / annual total hooks from the same A observations. ",
      "Any A effort filter applies to both curves.\n\n", sep = "")
}
```

## Relative indices for analyses A and B

```{r}
plot_series(series, "index", "CPUE relative to first year")
```

Each index is divided by its own first-year prediction (first year = 1). `r if (public) "A adjusts cell–quarter composition; B adjusts quarter only on the same fixed prediction support." else "A adjusts vessel effects; B uses year effects only."` B may use different records because the optional effort filter applies to A.

## Methods and checks

```{r}
#| results: asis
if (public) {
  cat("The saved models are quasipoisson GLMs with a log link and ",
      "a `log(hooks / 1000)` offset. A adjusts for cell–quarter composition; ",
      "B adjusts quarter only; quarter effects partly confound spatial differences on this support. The source rows are fleet–cell–quarter aggregates, ",
      "not individual fishing sets.\n\n", sep = "")
} else if (length(result$analyses)) {
  cat("The saved synthetic models are Poisson GLMs with a log link and ",
      "a `log(hooks / 1000)` offset. A adjusts for vessel effects; ",
      "B has year effects only.\n\n", sep = "")
}
if (length(result$analyses)) {
  for (i in seq_along(result$analyses)) {
    x <- result$analyses[[i]]
    cat("**", LETTERS[i], ":** `", x$formula, "`\n\n", sep = "")
  }
  if (!is.null(analysis_a$min_hooks)) {
    cat("A effort threshold: **", analysis_a$min_hooks, " hooks per record** ",
        "(0 retains all eligible records).\n\n", sep = "")
  }
  checks <- lapply(seq_along(result$analyses), function(i) {
    x <- result$analyses[[i]]
    row <- list(Analysis = LETTERS[i], Rows_used = x$sets_used,
                Excluded = x$sets_excluded, Converged = x$converged,
                Score_error = format(x$score_residual, scientific = TRUE, digits = 3))
    if (public) row$Dispersion <- x$dispersion
    row
  })
  print(knitr::kable(rows_frame(checks), digits = 3,
                    col.names = gsub("_", " ", names(checks[[1]]))))
  if (!public) {
    cat("\nScore error is the maximum relative difference between fitted and observed annual catch totals.\n\n")
  }
}
if (public && length(analysis_a$weights)) {
  weights <- vapply(analysis_a$weights, function(x) {
    paste0("`", x$stratum, "` (", format(x$weight, digits = 4), ")")
  }, character(1))
  cat("\nA's fixed prediction weights: ", paste(weights, collapse = "; "), ".\n\n",
      "Score error is the maximum relative difference between fitted and observed annual, stratum or quarter catch totals. ",
      "Dispersion is the Pearson sum of squares divided by residual degrees of freedom.\n\n", sep = "")
}
```

## Interpretation and limits

```{r}
#| results: asis
if (public) {
  cat("The first plot shows the effect of holding cell–quarter composition fixed for A's selected records. ",
      "The index covers the selected fleet and released strata; vessel identifiers are unavailable. ",
      "Released zero counts are retained, while absent or suppressed values are not zero catch. ",
      "An effort filter may require model predictions for omitted year–stratum combinations. ",
      "No confidence intervals are supplied, and this workflow example provides no stock-wide or official assessment advice.\n\n", sep = "")
} else {
  cat("The comparison illustrates vessel adjustment for the selected synthetic observations. ",
      "No confidence intervals or fisheries advice are supplied.\n\n", sep = "")
}
```

SHA-256: c5034f2d8c16433cea975939eae287a39e348b5373776df8057268f696815aed

# Preserve both index series and the actual method and prediction support.
calculate <- function(context) {
  analyses <- context$parents[c("cpue_a", "cpue_b")]
  titles <- c("CPUE analysis A", "CPUE analysis B")
  result <- compare_series(analyses, titles)
  result$analyses <- setNames(lapply(analyses, function(analysis) {
    analysis[setdiff(names(analysis), "series")]
  }), titles)
  result$data_kind <- analyses[[1]]$data_kind
  result
}

# QC is the scheduling gate; accepted submission rows are the calculation input.
# The coordinator writes these same rows to the SQLite snapshot.
calculate <- function(context) {
  accepted <- context$parents$submission
  years <- rows_values(accepted$sets, "year")
  summary <- describe_data(accepted$sets, accepted$catch)
  c(list(rows = length(years), first_year = min(years), last_year = max(years)), summary)
}

# Extract data (`extract`)

Use recorded SQLite queries to select the fixed CPUE support and annual catches.

**Path:** SQLite snapshot → recorded SQL → CPUE rows and annual catches.

**Inputs:** `runs/database/snapshot.sqlite`, [`extract.sql`](../../workflow/extract.sql) and [`extract-catch.sql`](../../workflow/extract-catch.sql).

**From:** [Prepare and load (`database`)](../database/README.md).

**Settings:** No job-specific settings (`{}`).

CPUE rows retain fleet, year, cell, quarter, hooks and albacore catch in numbers. Annual catch is a separate tonnes series. Released zero counts are retained; absent or suppressed values are not converted to zeros.

From the repository root, run this job and prepare any required inputs:

```sh
make job JOB=extract
```

SHA-256: 8c94e3379ec3360fb77c6fa216fa4ab67f0392040ddb842fc96e90a3a69759ce

# These rows must come from the coordinator's real snapshot SQLite queries.
calculate <- function(context) {
  extracted <- context$extracted
  require_condition(!is.null(extracted) && is.character(extracted$sql),
                    "The saved SQLite extraction input is missing.")
  check_sets(extracted$sets)
  check_catches(extracted$catch)
  result <- list(sets = extracted$sets, catch = extracted$catch, sql = extracted$sql)
  if (!is.null(extracted$data_kind)) result$data_kind <- extracted$data_kind
  if (!is.null(extracted$metadata)) result$metadata <- extracted$metadata
  result
}

SHA-256: cc33933f02ed22b76f1e373f2af315d2f88d169c8774d263cb9dff17ab67eafb

Compare catch, biomass and catch stability for three rules under the same simulated future conditions.

**Path:** Four fitted cases → shared trials and catch rules → management comparison.

```{r}
# Read saved results without fitting or simulating again.
payload <- jsonlite::fromJSON("report-data.json", simplifyVector = FALSE)
result <- payload$result
record <- payload$record
rows_frame <- function(rows) {
  do.call(rbind, lapply(rows, function(row) as.data.frame(row, stringsAsFactors = FALSE)))
}
plot_series <- function(series, field, label) {
  years <- unlist(lapply(series, function(rows) vapply(rows, function(x) x$year, numeric(1))))
  values <- unlist(lapply(series, function(rows) vapply(rows, function(x) x[[field]], numeric(1))))
  colours <- c("#1378a3", "#b56435", "#7357a8", "#328363")
  old_mar <- par("mar")
  on.exit(par(mar = old_mar))
  par(mar = c(4, 4, 5, 1))
  plot(range(years), range(c(0, values)), type = "n", xlab = "Year", ylab = label)
  for (i in seq_along(series)) {
    lines(vapply(series[[i]], function(x) x$year, numeric(1)),
          vapply(series[[i]], function(x) x[[field]], numeric(1)),
          col = colours[(i - 1) %% length(colours) + 1], lwd = 2)
  }
  legend("top", names(series), inset = c(0, -0.34), xpd = NA,
         col = colours[seq_along(series)], lty = 1, lwd = 2, cex = 0.8, bty = "n")
}
```

## Management comparison

```{r}
# Use the recorded rule order for consistent colours in every plot.
rule_names <- vapply(result$metrics, function(row) row$name, character(1))
plot_series(result$series[rule_names], "B_over_K", "Median end-year biomass / carrying capacity")
```

Compare biomass alongside realised catch and annual catch changes. These
conditional trials provide neither calibrated risk estimates nor evidence that
a rule would be safe without the shared harvest guardrail. Fixed case differences
do not include assessment parameter uncertainty or select a rule for a real fishery.

```{r}
#| results: asis
if (length(result$boundary_cases)) {
  cat("**Review needed:** assessment search boundaries were reached for ",
      paste(unlist(result$boundary_cases), collapse = ", "), ".\n\n", sep = "")
}
if (any(vapply(result$metrics, function(row) row$shortfall_trials > 0, logical(1)))) {
  cat("Some requested catches exceeded the harvest guardrail. Metrics use realised catches.\n\n")
}
```

### Catch, biomass and catch stability

```{r}
metrics <- lapply(result$metrics, function(row) {
  list(rule = row$name, mean_catch_t = row$mean_catch_t,
       final_B_over_K = row$final_B_over_K,
       below_threshold_percent = row$below_threshold_percent,
       catch_change_percent = row$catch_change_percent)
})
knitr::kable(rows_frame(metrics), digits = 3,
             col.names = c("Rule", "Mean catch (t/year)", "Final B/K",
                           "Trials below 0.2 (%)", "Catch change (%)"))
```

Mean catch is in tonnes per year; final B/K is the median across trials. The
threshold column counts trials below `r result$assumptions$depletion_threshold`
of carrying capacity at any time. Catch change averages each trial's `100 × sum(abs(C_t − C_(t−1))) / sum(C_t)` using realised catches,
including the first change from the reference catch. Cases and scenarios have equal weight, not estimated probabilities.



### Trajectories by growth scenario

```{r}
par(mfrow = c(1, 2), mar = c(4, 4, 3, 1))
for (scenario in result$scenarios) {
  series <- scenario$series[rule_names]
  label <- sub(" growth$", "", sub(" and recovery$", "", scenario$name))
  plot_series(series, "B_over_K", paste0(label, ": median B/K"))
  plot_series(series, "catch_t", paste0(label, ": median catch (t)"))
}
```



### Trial assumptions and limits

One scenario keeps the growth multiplier at one. The other uses 0.7 for six
years, then returns to one. Mean-one lognormal growth and observation errors
have coefficients of variation `r result$assumptions$growth_cv` and
`r result$assumptions$observation_cv` before the growth cap is applied. The
preparation result preserves seeds, RNG settings and actual paired error vectors.
Surplus production uses starting biomass; realised catch is then removed.

```{r}
#| results: asis
for (limitation in result$limitations) cat("- ", limitation, "\n", sep = "")
```




## Methods


```{r}
#| results: asis
if (!is.null(record$data_source)) {
  cat("**Source coverage:** ", record$data_source$cpue_scope,
      ". **Annual catch:** ", record$data_source$catch_scope, ".\n\n", sep = "")
  cat("The selected Fiji fleet index is not a validated stock-wide abundance index. ",
      "Using it with stock-wide catches is a teaching assumption.\n\n", sep = "")
} else {
  cat("The supplied index and catches define this teaching example; ",
      "inspect the producing records for their data scope.\n\n", sep = "")
}
```

Four fitted Schaefer cases supply starting biomass, capacity, growth and
catchability. Constant catch, an index rule and a buffered rule use the same
saved growth and observation errors for **`r result$assumptions$years` years**,
with **`r result$assumptions$replicates` trials** per case and scenario.
Each year, observe an index → set catch → update biomass → observe again.
The index rules use a three-year observed mean. Reference catch C₀ and index
I₀ are the last three historical-year means. Rₜ is the mean of the current
simulated index and previous two indices, divided by I₀.

| Rule | Requested catch |
|:--|:--|
| Constant | C₀ |
| Index | C₀ × min(`r result$assumptions$maximum_catch_multiple`, Rₜ) |
| Buffered | b × C₀ × m(Rₜ), limited to ±`r 100 * result$assumptions$annual_change_limit`% of the previous request (initially C₀) |

Here b is `r result$rules$buffered$settings$buffer`. The step multiplier m is
`r paste(unlist(result$assumptions$buffered_steps$multipliers), collapse = ", ")`
for Rₜ below `r result$assumptions$buffered_steps$thresholds[[1]]`, between
`r paste(unlist(result$assumptions$buffered_steps$thresholds), collapse = " and ")`,
or at least `r result$assumptions$buffered_steps$thresholds[[2]]`, respectively.

**Shared guardrail:** realised catch is capped at
`r 100 * result$assumptions$maximum_harvest_rate`% of true starting biomass.
This simulator safeguard uses biomass unavailable to the index rules; every
rule's biomass and low-biomass statistics are conditional on that guardrail.
Growth is capped at `r result$assumptions$maximum_growth_rate` per year.

## Analysis record

Produced in **`r record$run_id`**. Retained inputs keep their original producing runs.

```{r}
#| results: asis
if (!is.null(record$execution$provider)) {
  cat("Execution: ", record$execution$provider, ".\n\n", sep = "")
}
if (knitr::is_html_output()) {
  cat("Calculation image: `", record$software$container, "`\n\n", sep = "")
} else {
  digest <- sub(".*@sha256:", "", record$software$container)
  cat("Calculation image: `sha256:", substr(digest, 1, 12), "…` (full digest in the run bundle).\n\n", sep = "")
}
if (!is.null(record$software$container_url)) {
  cat("[Container package](", record$software$container_url, ")\n\n", sep = "")
}
for (key in c("source", "analysis_source")) {
  source <- record[[key]]
  if (!is.null(source$repository) && !is.null(source$commit)) {
    cat("[", key, " at ", substr(source$commit, 1, 8), "](",
        source$repository, "/commit/", source$commit, ")\n\n", sep = "")
  }
}
```

Annual values are available in the **Data** tab. Input files, settings, source versions and checksums are available in **Inputs & versions** and the downloaded run. Rendering reads saved results and does not fit models again.

# Prepare inputs B (`prepare_b`)

SHA-256: 9737e611cdc352101b32c8b7e829e9fedb6842a5a2365da48735ba021a2c71bc

# Correct invalid synthetic effort from the raw supplied rows, if required.
calculate <- function(context) {
  submission <- context$parents$submission
  invalid <- Filter(function(row) row$hooks <= 0 || row$catch_n < 0, submission$sets)
  returned <- json_array(rows_values(invalid, "set_id"))
  if (length(invalid) > 0) {
    submission <- source_rows(context$source, context$settings$last_year)
  }
  check_sets(submission$sets)
  public <- !is.null(submission$sets[[1]]$stratum)
  result <- list(returned = returned, rows = length(submission$sets), checks = list(
    list(check = "Positive effort", result = "Pass"),
    list(check = "Non-negative released catch", result = "Pass"),
    list(check = if (public) "Unique aggregate source-row IDs" else "Unique observation IDs", result = "Pass")))
  if (length(invalid) > 0) {
    attr(result, "workflow_effects") <- list(resubmit_submission = submission)
  }
  result
}

# Assemble supplied records; the invalid-effort correction is a synthetic example.
calculate <- function(context) {
  result <- source_rows(context$source, context$settings$last_year)
  require_condition(length(result$sets) > 0, "No submission records supplied.")
  if (is.null(result$sets[[1]]$stratum)) result$sets[[1]]$hooks <- 0
  result
}
run.py


SHA-256: ca046838b2f7d231d66f06f47f07cb957edb2ffd1d70b7484d27720ed5ce90c6

"""Run the preserved example with Python's standard library."""
import argparse
import asyncio
import json
from pathlib import Path

from workflow.engine import Workflow
from workflow.spec import SPEC


def main():
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)
    parser.add_argument('--from', dest='start', choices=SPEC, default='submission')
    parser.add_argument('--scope', choices=['workflow', 'job'], default='workflow',
                        help='Run the downstream workflow, or only the selected job and its required inputs.')
    parser.add_argument('--output', default='runs')
    parser.add_argument('--settings', type=Path)
    args = parser.parse_args()
    workflow = Workflow(args.output, notify=lambda e: print(e.get('job','Workflow'), e['state'], e.get('message',''), flush=True))
    if args.settings:
        workflow.configure(json.loads(args.settings.read_text()))
    result = asyncio.run(workflow.run(args.start, args.scope))
    print(f"{result['run_id']}: {len(result['run'])} jobs executed; {len(result['retained'])} retained.")
    report = args.start if args.scope == 'job' else 'assessment_report'
    print(Path(args.output, report, 'report.html').resolve())


if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()

# Repeat the bounded repository checks

The default coordinator selects the exact commits in `source-lock.template.json`.
In a Git checkout, `python3 scripts/hydrate-sources.py` verifies pins and assembles source
bytes before the network-disabled calculation container starts. Local validation
can use `--local-root ../component-repositories`; the coordinator must be clean
at its actual committed executable revision. `coordinator-origin.json` binds that
revision to the archived coordinator source bytes. This is separate from a later
commit delivering generated site files. Extracted portable archives already carry
verified snapshots: use `python3 scripts/hydrate-sources.py --if-needed` to check
them without a Git checkout or a new network retrieval.

The optional `Dockerfile` embeds the preserved monorepo compatibility mode.
Default split execution uses the SPC image with the hydrated checkout mounted.

Run `python3 scripts/check-multi-repository.py --plan` to inspect the finite
MR-01–08 inventory without calculations. Numerical execution requires the same
preserved SPC image and a fresh output directory. The parent supplies a pinned
monorepo reference and separately hydrated validation source locks. For complete
offline MR-01–08 repetition, a companion archive must include that reference, its
manifest, and both variant snapshots; an ordinary downloaded run contains its
own current component sources. Run the command inside the preserved container
with its exact digest in `PAPER_RUNTIME_IMAGE` and supply both variant options:

```sh
python3 scripts/check-multi-repository.py \
  --source-lock source-lock.json \
  --reference-root /reference \
  --reference-manifest /reference-manifest.json \
  --code-source-lock /variants/mr04/source-lock.json \
  --failure-source-lock /variants/mr08/source-lock.json \
  --output /outputs/multi-repository \
  --timeout 180
```

A fresh full invocation declares18 attempts. An explicit continuation from the
preserved first FAILED receipt imports only its six completed MR-01–03 attempts
and declares12 remaining actual attempts. Imported outputs and producing records
keep their genuine earlier source/execution pins; they are never recalculated. Apply an outer container wall limit as well as
its cooperative per-attempt limit: synchronous Quarto rendering has its own
finite process timeout. Preserve failed/partial receipts; execution is never
replayed automatically. Record exact source/input/output hashes, the command,
container digest and CPU/memory limits before interpreting the result.

MR-04 changes only the Buffered job expression from
`buffer = context$settings$mse_buffer` to
`buffer = min(context$settings$mse_buffer, 0.6)` at fixed configuration. MR-08 uses a
separate commit that deliberately stops that job. These validation branches do
not change the default component's scientific source. The corrected MR-04 expression selects the supported0.6 fraction at the fixed
0.8 setting. The first attempted expression produced an unsupported0.72 and
failed; its original receipt and source commit remain preserved. The runner
verifies that all other contributing component bytes are unchanged. Without supplied locks,
its optional variant helper needs Git and creates isolated local test commits;
container execution can instead use the parent-precommitted snapshot locks.

MR-05 injects malformed transferred file bytes with explicitly labelled
fixture-only checkpoint checksums. It preserves and restores the real producer
record and output before corrected calls. MR-06 checks missing/tampered files,
MR-07 checks stale producing material, and MR-08 keeps earlier reports identifiable
while testing failure and recovery. None of these injected adverse fixtures is
attributed to an unobserved operational fisheries event.

`evidence.json` records attempted cases, retained producing pins, comparison
results and output hashes. The immutable comparator remains `verify.py` with its
existing metric-specific rules. Downloaded runs include this runner, guide,
component source snapshots and origins, so the actual files can be inspected and
repeated offline when the pinned runtime image is already available. Complete
MR-01–08 repetition also needs the reference and variant companion sources above.
Successful conformance does not approve model adequacy,
management advice, operational adoption or a performance improvement.


For an explicit checkpoint continuation, supply the corrected MR-04 snapshot and a fresh
output directory. Append these explicit checkpoint options to the command above:

```sh
--continue-from /prior/evidence.json \
--continue-sha256 c1e545a9faacc19ea04d13eeab510f3bbb66846b54c47414e461a03752b09f22 \
--checkpoint-origin /prior-source/coordinator-origin.json \

```

The preserved coordinator origin must name executable commit
`5cca42bedf3ea61027b282799400adf8483980b9`. The new executable commit is separately
hydrated. All analytical/core source and input hashes must match the original;
only this proof runner, its two software-test files, this guide and the optional
Dockerfile compatibility declaration may differ. Both supplied component variant
locks and the exact monorepo reference manifest remain mandatory. The validator
checks the original FAILED receipt, complete artifact membership/bytes, settings,
producing records, signatures and image before any new case. It copies the six
completed output directories into the new evidence root, rechecks their recorded
bytes and existing-output comparisons, and records explicit import provenance.
The failed first MR-04 remains linked through its immutable prior receipt;
continuation is selected by the parent and never happens automatically.

The public companion supplies the mono reference/manifest, both component
variants and a pinned coordinator closure for a fresh18-attempt repetition.

"""Finite MR-01--08 conformance evidence; execute only in the pinned container.

The --plan operation performs no calculations. The numerical operation uses the
real Workflow/R/Quarto paths, including deliberate invalid inputs and one actual

from workflow.r_bridge import RBridge
from workflow.spec import SPEC, DEFAULTS
from verify import compare, verify

FAILURE_TEXT = 'MR08 intentional Buffered simulation failure'
ATTEMPTS = (
    ('MR-01', 'reference-baseline', 'reference subprocess', ALL),
    ('MR-01', 'split-baseline', 'workflow', ALL),
    ('MR-02', 'cpue-selective', 'workflow', CPUE),
    ('MR-02', 'cpue-fresh', 'workflow', ALL),
    ('MR-03', 'buffer-selective', 'workflow', BUFFER),
